The scheduler would leave them with the processor, between the convolution and the gates, so `llama_context::graph_get_cb` (`src/llama-context.cpp`) gives an accelerator that takes them an RMS_NORM followed by a SCALE of a layer of the processor; and so that the norms and the recurrence are one part of the graph, the backend also takes the gates of a batch (`fGateTaken`: alpha + `ssm_dt`, its SOFTPLUS, the MUL by `ssm_a`, and the SIGMOID of beta), computed on the host value by value as the CPU backend does (`fGateOnHost`: `op_softplus` and `op_sigmoid` with `expf` and `log1pf`), in pieces for the threads: on one thread the gates took what the norms saved. `GGML_XDNA_NO_L2` leaves norms and gates with the CPU backend.
 - **The outputs by rows** (`fXdnaGdnUnpack`): every task takes a round, a group, a head of keys and one of its two value heads, and writes every row of 128 values of that value head whole (its 16 blocks of 8 values come from 16 objects of C: 8 columns by 2 rows), past the cache; before, every task wrote one block of 8 values of 128 rows, 16 KB apart. The same values. With `llama-xdna-gemm gdn --tokens 512`, reading C took 1.35 and 1.37 ms instead of 2.31 and 2.34 (alternating).
 - **Checked**: the logits identical byte for byte to those of before both changes (batches of 512), the same text of 128 tokens, and the recurrence of the tool within 0.0008 of the reference in its outputs.
 - **Measured** with the laptop in its slow state of that evening (below): the batch of 512 at 826 and 820 tokens per second against 806 and 803 with `GGML_XDNA_NO_L2` (+2.4 %, both with the outputs by rows).
 - **The laptop slowed down by a third that evening** after many hours of measurements, with nothing to show for it: the processor alone did a batch of 512 at 101 tokens per second against 153 an hour before, the backend at about 810 against about 1,260, and the parts bound by memory were slower still (the recurrence of the tool read C in 2.3 ms against 0.82 earlier that day); plugged in, in the profile `performance`, with boost on, the processor at 47 °C, the memory at 46 °C and the system idle. Only interleaved rounds compare.

**Towards the attention on the NPU: what it is worth, what it costs in precision, and where it can live** (2026/11/16, point 3 of the three the user confirmed). The attention of Qwen3.5 4B: 8 layers of full attention, 16 heads of queries and 4 of keys and values of 256 (GQA 4:1), RMS norms of q and k, multi-section RoPE, a sigmoid gate on its output; ggml computes it with `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` over the cache (F16), on the processor by tiles of 64 queries by 64 keys (`ggml_compute_forward_flash_attn_ext_tiled`).

 - **What it is worth**: with the context already in the cache it is what grows. A batch of 512 of the 4B Q4_K_M with the NPU (`llama-bench -p 512 -d 0,2048,8192`, the laptop in its slow state): 1,089 tokens per second with no context, 709 with 2,048 tokens before it and 295 with 8,192: some 0.47, 0.72 and 1.74 s a batch, nearly all the growth the attention of the processor (some 1.3 s with 8,192, some 0.23 T multiply-adds a second). The NPU would do those 290 G multiply-adds in some tens of milliseconds.
 - **What block floating point costs**: measured with a temporary emulation in the attention of the processor (its operands rounded as `fXdnaBfp16Encode` rounds them, by blocks of 8 values: Q and K along the dimension of a head, P and V along the keys, as two products of the overlay of the prompts would take them; removed afterwards, and the logits of the processor alone are again identical byte for byte to `backend-test/cpu-orig.kld`). Against the processor with Qwen3.5 0.8B in F32 and a context of 2,048 (`-c 2048 -b 512 --chunks 1`): Q and K in bfp16, KL 0.000042 and the same token in 99.51 %; Q, K, P and V in bfp16, 0.000076 and 99.12 %, against some 0.0011 that bfp16 costs in the multiplications by weights. Plain bfp16 is enough, with no splitting. With a quantized model the comparison does not work: any change of the attention, even with Q and K in two parts (some 14 bits), gave a KL of about 0.002 with the 4B Q4_K_M, because the processor rounds the activations of its multiplications to 8 bits and that amplifies the least difference; without the emulation the KL is 0.000001.
 - **Where it can live**: not in the overlay of the prompts, whose cores use 15,536 of their 16,384 bytes of program with the multiplication and the recurrence. In an overlay of its own, a change of overlay costs some 2.5 ms each way: some 40 ms a batch for the 8 layers, so it pays only with a long context (some 30 ms of the processor per layer with 2,560 keys, some 160 with 8,700). That was the conclusion of the study; what was built goes another way (next section).

### The attention of a batch on the NPU, as two multiplications

Built on 2026/11/16 and on by default (`GGML_XDNA_NO_ATTN` gives it back to the processor). There is no kernel or overlay of its own: the attention of a batch (`FLASH_ATTN_EXT` of ggml) is two multiplications on the overlay of the prompts for every head of keys and values, and the softmax goes on the host between them, as the SwiGLU already went into the rows of `ffn_down`. No change of overlay, and no new design that could hang the NPU.

 - **The two products**: S = Q·Kᵀ, with the queries of the 4 heads that share the head of keys (GQA 4:1) as its rows, by tokens (row r is the token r / 4 of the head r % 4), Q times the scale of the attention before it is converted (1/16 with heads of 256: a power of two, exact), and the keys as the weights. Then O = P·V, with P = exp(S + mask − the largest of its row) converted straight into the buffers of A of the second multiplication, V transposed as its weights, in pieces of at most 4,096 keys (the inputs of one multiplication), and O divided by the sum of every row of P, taken before P is rounded. A block of rows is at most 2,048 (512 tokens of 4 heads). K and the pieces of V of a head are converted once from the F16 cache into buffers that are kept (`XdnaShared::vAttnK`, `aAttnV`), for all its blocks of tokens.
 - **Precision**: S comes back from the NPU in bfloat16, which the emulation of the study did not have. The same emulation with S in bfloat16 (Qwen3.5 0.8B in F32, `llama-perplexity -c 2048 -b 512 --chunks 4 -fa on`, against the processor): Q, K, P and V in bfp16, KL 0.000074; with S in bfloat16, 0.000114; with S and O in bfloat16, 0.000120; the same token in 99.44 % in the three. On the NPU, against the same reference: 0.001072 with the attention on the NPU and 0.000976 with it on the processor (0.000096 more, what the emulation predicted), the same token in 98.27 and 98.31 %; with batches of 200 tokens, 0.001074.
 - **The mask**: the KQ mask of llama.cpp is 0 or minus infinity. `fXdnaAttnMaskKinds` sorts every strip of 64 keys of every token into all masked, all 0 or mixed, so that a masked strip is not read and an open one adds no mask.
 - **The host**: P is computed by bands of 64 rows (`fXdnaGemmPackAttnP`): the largest score of each row from all its strips, then its tiles of P piece after piece in the order of the keys, with the vector exp of ggml (`fXdnaScoresExp`), and the sums of the rows in that order. Computing it tile by tile (the first version) took the same: it is bound by the arithmetic, not by reading S twice.
 - **The encoder**: with the attention, the encoder of bfp16 took 17 % of the cycles of the whole process (`perf record -e cycles:u`). Its AVX2 path now takes the mantissas of a block whose largest exponent is 8 or more as the value times 2 to the power of (133 − that exponent), converted with the rounding of the processor (`_mm256_cvtps_epi32`): the product by a power of two is exact (it is under 128, and what falls under the normal floats is far under a half), ties go to the even one as in the integers, and the packs hold +128 at +127. The same bytes as before (logits of Qwen3.5 0.8B BF16 on the NPU identical byte for byte with and without it), with 3 operations instead of some 15. P from S went from 1,215 to 903 ms, and converting Q, K and V from 431 to 360 ms, in the 160 attentions of a measure with 8,192 tokens of context.
 - **Overlap**: while the NPU multiplies P by V of a head, the host converts the keys, the values and the queries of the next one and sends its S, which the NPU computes while the host reads O. The values go in two sets of buffers. With more than 12,288 keys (4 pieces, all the buffers of C) it goes one after the other, and so with `GGML_XDNA_NO_ATTN_OVERLAP` or `GGML_XDNA_NO_OVERLAP`. Logits identical byte for byte with and without it (0.8B BF16, a context of 8,192, two pieces). With 8,192 tokens of context: the attention from 2,686 and 2,730 ms to 2,392 and 2,428 ms; the wait for the NPU halves, and the host gets some 10 % slower while the NPU reads memory.
 - **The planner**: llama.cpp only moves an operation to an accelerator from its weights or from the nodes next to it, and the attention has neither: `llama_context::graph_get_cb` (`src/llama-context.cpp`) gives it (behind the `RESHAPE` that `build_attn` names `kqv_out`) to an accelerator that takes it on a layer of the processor. The probe of `resolve_fused_ops` builds it with 1 token, which stays with the processor, so flash attention stays on.
 - **What it takes**: as many tokens as a multiplication of the prompts (`GGML_XDNA_MIN_BATCH`, 40), keys and values in F16, at most 16,128 keys (the outputs of one multiplication; a longer context goes on the processor), optional attention sinks (`src[4]`, in F32, one per head; since 2026/11/18), and no ALiBi or soft cap.

 - **Q and K in two parts when the queries are not normalised** (`fNormedQueries`, `fXdnaAttnSplit`, `XdnaAttnRows::vSplit`, the `pSplitDim` of `fXdnaGemmPackBHalves`; `GGML_XDNA_ATTN_SPLIT=0|1` forces it). With Granite 4.2 3B (no norm of q and k, heads of 64) the attention on the NPU added a KL of 0.0041 against the processor with the same weights in F16 (0.0038 with the attention on the processor, 0.0080 on the NPU). The same emulation as above, with Granite: S in bfloat16, 0.000019; P and V in bfp16, 0.00048; Q and K in bfp16, 0.0039: a few dimensions of q and k are much larger than the others, and the others lose their bits in the blocks of 8 they share with them. Qwen3.5 normalises q and k (its RMS norms), and there it costs 0.000042. So when the queries do not come from an RMS norm (through views and the RoPE), Q goes as [high, high, low] and K as [high, low, high] of their two parts of bfp16 (the part that a block keeps, and what is left), and one multiplication of three times the dimension gives Qh·Khᵀ + Qh·Klᵀ + Ql·Khᵀ, some 14 bits. With Granite: KL 0.004331 and the same token in 97.19 %, as with the attention on the processor; with heads of 64 the 192 inputs fit in the 256 the NPU takes anyway. Its conversions cost some 2.6 times the others, so then the attention needs 1,024 keys or more (`cAttnSplitMinKeys`).
 - **Blocks of rows**: at most the rows of one command (4,096). With 2,048, the 5 heads of a group of Granite cut a batch of 512 in two, twice the commands: with no context the attention on the NPU went from 31 % to 19 % slower than the processor.

Qwen3.5 4B Q4_K_M, `llama-bench -p 512 -n 0 -d <context> -fa 1`, rounds interleaved, attention on the processor / on the NPU (tokens per second):

| Context | Processor | NPU |
|---|---|---|
| none | 1,089 and 1,083 | 1,149 and 1,150 (+6 %) |
| 512 | 925 and 923 | 1,127 and 1,144 (+23 %) |
| 1,024 | 793 and 786 | 1,118 and 1,118 (+41 %) |
| 2,048 | 696 and 680 | 1,048 and 1,045 (+52 %, before the encoder and the overlap) |
| 8,192 | 294 and 293 | 810 and 799 (2.7 times, the same); 829 and 819 with the overlap |

Where the time goes with 8,192 tokens of context (160 attentions, the report of the backend with `-v`): NPU 377 ms (the wait, with the overlap), converting Q, K and V 428, P from S 912, reading O 343, flushing the cache 341. The host is what is left: at that context S is 35.7 MB for every head of keys of a layer and P 20 MB, some 1.1 GB and 0.6 GB a batch through the memory, and the host computes an exponential for every score. A kernel of flash attention on the NPU would keep S and P in the cores, at the cost of an overlay of its own.

### Small batches, and the weights in the mapping of the file

Measured on 2026/11/16, after the attention of a batch went to the NPU.

 - **The threshold of rows** (`GGML_XDNA_MIN_BATCH`): it was 72, from Qwen3.5 0.8B and the overlay of 2026/11/13. Measured again with batches of 16 to 64 tokens (`llama-bench -p ... -n 0 -r 3`, two rounds interleaved, the processor alone against the NPU from 9 rows on, tokens per second):

| Batch | 4B Q4_K_M, processor | 4B Q4_K_M, NPU | 0.8B Q8_0, processor | 0.8B Q8_0, NPU |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 16 | 121 and 112 | 75 and 76 | | |
| 32 | 150 and 143 | 132 and 132 | 542 and 480 | 437 and 433 |
| 40 | 138 and 137 | 165 and 166 | 519 and 494 | 551 and 543 |
| 48 | 161 and 158 | 187 and 191 | 512 and 500 | 644 and 630 |
| 64 | 170 and 169 | 265 and 268 | 505 and 500 | 760 and 784 |

   In both the crossing is between 32 and 40 tokens, and the threshold is now 40. From 9 to 39 rows the multiplications stay with the processor. Sending them to the chained overlay in commands of up to 8 rows was tried and was slower than the processor (16 rows: 92.7 against 121 tokens per second): every command reads all the weights, the multiplications go one by one instead of a layer in a command, and a batch of 16 takes some 300 commands. It was taken out.
 - **The weights in the mapping of the file** (`fDeviceBufferFromHostPtr`, the capability `buffer_from_host_ptr`): when the model is mapped (the default of llama.cpp), the buffers of weights of this backend are made over the mapping of the file, as the CPU backend keeps them, instead of allocated and filled with a copy. The original weights are then pages of the file, which the system can take back, and the only anonymous memory of the weights is what the NPU reads. Qwen3.5 4B Q4_K_M with `llama-perplexity`: the largest anonymous memory resident (RssAnon) went from 3.54 to 1.03 GiB, with the logits identical byte for byte and the same text of 128 tokens generated. `GGML_XDNA_COPY_WEIGHTS=1` copies them as before. What the NPU reads stays twice: in bfp16 for the overlay of the prompts and in the format of the file for the chained overlay (shared memory of the driver, outside RssAnon).
 - **Changes of overlay**: the runner counts the commands that run on the other overlay than the command before (`XdnaRunner::mOverlaySwitches`), and the backend says how many there were when it is freed. With batches of 16 to 128 tokens and `llama-bench`, some 2 a batch (from the overlay of the prompts to the chained one for the logits of the last token, and back).

### Granite 4.2 3B on the backend

Measured on 2026/11/16 with `granite-4.2-3b-Q5_K_M.gguf` of `ibm-granite/granite-4.2-3b-GGUF` (SHA-256 `982e7974…886c5efe`, in `~/IA/Modelos/GGUF/`): 40 layers, 40 heads of queries and 8 of keys and values of 64, no norm of q and k, an attention scale of 1/64. The fusions of the backend that look for the patterns of Qwen3.5 leave it alone, and the rest goes as with Qwen3.5.

 - **Precision**, against the processor with the same weights in F16 (`llama-quantize --allow-requantize --pure --output-tensor-type f16 --token-embedding-type f16`, then removed; `llama-perplexity -c 2048 -b 512 --chunks 4 -fa on`): the processor with the Q5_K_M file, KL 0.0113 and the same token in 95.21 %; the NPU with the attention on the processor, 0.0038 and 97.17 %; the NPU with the attention on the NPU, Q and K in two parts, 0.0043 and 97.19 %. The NPU is closer to the weights than the processor, which rounds its activations to 8 bits. The text of 64 tokens of all three parts where two tokens almost tie (after 40 to 43 bytes).
 - **Speed** (`llama-bench -fa 1`, tokens per second): a batch of 512, the processor alone 212, the NPU 990 (4.7 times); generating, 16.5 and 18.7. With context (attention on the processor / on the NPU, two rounds): 984 and 986 / 988 and 981 with no context (on the processor, under 1,024 keys), 421 and 418 / 582 and 580 with 1,024, 167 and 165 / 282 and 281 with 4,096.

### The short convolution while C is read

Since 2026/11/16 (point 3 of the pending tasks, what is left of a prompt on the host). In a batch, the short convolution of a layer of linear attention of Qwen3.5 (its `SSM_CONV` by tokens and the `SILU` after it) is computed by the backend while it reads the C of the projection `attn_qkv`, instead of the backend writing the `CONCAT` of the old states and the tokens (16.9 MB a layer with the 4B) for the CPU backend to read it again.

 - **Where it was** (`GGML_SCHED_DEBUG=2 -lv 5`): the `SSM_CONV` and the `SILU` were in a part of the graph of the processor, kept apart from the part of the NPU (the projections and the `CONCAT`) by the copy of the new states of the convolution into the cache (`CPY`) and, in the first batch of a sequence, by the clearing and the gather of the recurrent state (`SCALE` and `GET_ROWS` of `build_rs`).
 - **The graph**: with the convolution by tokens, `src/models/qwen35.cpp` builds the convolution and its SiLU, and puts them into the graph, before the recurrent state, so that `build_rs` puts its nodes after them. The operations and their values are the same: the logits of the processor alone are identical byte for byte to those of before.
 - **What the backend takes** (`fConvTaken`): the `SSM_CONV` by tokens of a batch of `GGML_XDNA_MIN_BATCH` tokens or more, with 4 taps and channels that are a multiple of 16, its `SILU`, and that `CPY`. The arithmetic is that of the CPU backend (`ggml_compute_forward_ssm_conv_f32_by_tokens`): 4 fused multiply-adds from 0, the oldest tap first, and the SiLU of `ggml_v_silu`; every value goes through the lane of a vector, as on the CPU backend when the channels are a multiple of its vectors, so the floats are the same (`fXdnaConvToken`, `fXdnaConvRowsBf16` and `fXdnaSilu` in `xdna-glu.cpp`).
 - **With the projection** (`fConvFold` in `ggml-xdna.cpp`, `fConvStrips` in `xdna-gemm.cpp`): when, in the part of the graph, only the `CPY`, the `SSM_CONV` and the `SILU` come after the `CONCAT`, nothing else reads the `CONCAT`, and the multiplication goes in one command of rows and in one piece along its inputs (a row needs the 3 rows before it, and the whole sums), the C of `attn_qkv` is read strip by strip: for every row, the 4 rows of its window as bfloat16 converted in the registers (the 3 before the first from the old states, copied before the multiplication writes anything), the result past the cache into the `SILU`, and the last 3 rows as they are into the `CONCAT`, for the `CPY`. The `SILU` is then written before its turn in the graph, so its memory has to be apart from what the nodes up to it read or write (the other results of the multiplications, the `CONCAT` and the cell of the cache): that is checked with the addresses of the tensors, and otherwise the convolution goes on the host after the `CONCAT` (`fConvOnHost`, with the same arithmetic).
 - **Checked**: the logits on the NPU identical byte for byte with the convolution on the processor (`GGML_XDNA_NO_CONV=1`) and with the convolution while C is read, with Qwen3.5 0.8B BF16 in batches of 512 (144 convolutions) and in batches of 200, 200 and 112 (216), and with Qwen3.5 4B Q4_K_M in batches of 512 (96); the same with the convolution on the host (`GGML_XDNA_NO_CONV_FOLD=1`, 216 in batches of 200).
 - **Measured** (Qwen3.5 4B Q4_K_M, `llama-bench -p 512 -n 0 -r 5 -fa 1`, four rounds one after the other after a run left out, since the first run after a while idle comes out faster whatever it runs): 423.6 ms a batch against 438.8 with `GGML_XDNA_NO_CONV` (1,209 against 1,167 tokens per second, +3.5 %), faster in every round. The first version converted every value of C four times in a scalar loop (the library is built for baseline x86-64) and gained 1.5 %: its reading of C was some 0.6 ms a layer slower, about what the processor saved. Writing the `SILU` through the cache was not faster than past it (some 428 against 424 ms a batch). Then every strip goes in one call of the vectors for all its rows from the fourth (`fXdnaConvRowsBf16`; before, one call a row and a strip, 1.9 % of the cycles of the processor in `perf` only in choosing the vectors): logits identical again, and in a later session with the laptop slower, 438.8 against 451.3 ms a batch without the convolution (+2.8 %, three rounds).

### A mixture of experts: Qwen3.6 35B-A3B

Since 2026/11/17 (point 9 of the pending tasks of that day). The fork loads `qwen35moe`, Qwen3.5 with a block of experts instead of the dense feed-forward block: Qwen3.6 35B-A3B, 40 layers (30 of linear attention and 10 of attention, as `qwen35`) and a layer of multi-token prediction, 256 routed experts of 512 with 8 used by every token, and a shared expert of 512 with a gate of its own (one sigmoid per token).

 - **The port.** The fork had taken out everything of the mixtures of experts, so this brought back from upstream (llama.cpp 1.0.0) only what `qwen35moe` uses: the keys `expert_count`, `expert_used_count`, `expert_feed_forward_length` and `expert_shared_feed_forward_length` and three more, the tensors of the router, the experts and the shared expert with the operation that places their buffers (`MUL_MAT_ID` for the experts, and its test in `weight_buft_supported`), their parameters (`n_expert`, `n_expert_used`, `n_ff_exp`, `n_ff_shexp`; checked with exceptions on load, as the rest in this fork), `build_lora_mm_id` and a `build_moe_ffn` without the parts of other architectures (no biases, groups of experts, cache of experts, clamped SwiGLU or weights before the experts). `src/models/qwen35moe.cpp` is the `qwen35.cpp` of the fork, with everything it has for the NPU, and the changes of upstream between its `qwen35.cpp` and `qwen35moe.cpp`: the experts in the layers and in the layer of multi-token prediction, and the types of size.
 - **What goes where.** The experts (`MUL_MAT_ID`) and the router stay with the processor: the router would choose other experts if its logits came from block floating point (`fIsRouter`, by the names `ffn_gate_inp` and `ffn_gate_inp_shexp`), and its 256 outputs cost little. The rest of a layer goes to the NPU as with `qwen35`: in a batch, the projections, the short convolution while C is read, the recurrence, the attention, the shared expert with its SwiGLU into the rows of its down projection. The SwiGLU of the experts does not go to the backend (`fFromExperts`).
 - **Generating tokens goes with the processor, but the logits.** The overlay for generating tokens cannot chain the layers of a block of experts (`fFeedForward` matches the dense feed-forward block), so the multiplications of a layer went one by one, about 240 commands a token, around the experts on the processor: 11.1 tokens per second against 15.7 to 16.0 with the processor alone. When the backend has been asked about a router while llama.cpp places the weights (`fSawExperts`), the multiplications of a few rows stay with the processor, but the logits, one matrix of 2048 by 248,320: 16.4 tokens per second (+3 % over generating on the processor). `GGML_XDNA_MOE_GEMV=1` sends them all to the NPU as before.
 - **Measured** (Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K, 29.2 GB, `llama-bench -p 512 -n 64 -r 3 -fa 1`): a prompt of 512 tokens at 283 tokens per second against 168 with the processor alone (1.7 times); generating, 16.4 against 15.7. On the NPU against the processor alone, KL 0.0093 with batches of 512 (the same top token in 97.0 %) and 0.0091 with batches of one token (96.6 %). The text can part where two tokens nearly tie: after «The capital of France is Paris», the NPU gives «.» 0.469 and «,» 0.449.
 - **What is left.** The experts are some 0.8 GB a token and most of the time of a batch; on 2026/11/19 they moved to the NPU on an overlay of their own (see [Routed experts on the NPU](#routed-experts-on-the-npu)), leaving the dense projections of a generated token on the processor until they are chained too. `flint` (Flint 40B-A2.5B) came after, with more of what the fork had taken out (see [Flint 40B-A2.5B: a sliding window, attention sinks and experts](#flint-40b-a25b-a-sliding-window-attention-sinks-and-experts)).

### A model larger than the memory left for its copies: Qwen3.8 27B

Since 2026/11/17. The NPU reads its own copies of the weights: in block floating point for the overlay of the prompts (1.125 bytes a weight) and, for the overlay for generating tokens, in the blocks of the file (Q4_K, Q5_K and Q6_K) or in block floating point. With Qwen3.8 27B Q6_K (22.4 GB; 64 layers and one of multi-token prediction, 5120 wide, 16 heads of keys and 48 of values in its linear attention) they would take some 52 GB beside its mapped file, more than the 46.7 GiB of this laptop.

 - **A budget for the copies** (`fPlaceWeights`, `fCopiesBytes`): when llama.cpp places the weights of a model, the backend adds what their copies will take; a matrix that would go past `GGML_XDNA_WEIGHTS_GB` (by default 40 % of the memory of the machine, 19.1 GB here) stays with the processor, and so do the ones after it, so that the layers on the NPU are whole. Every matrix is counted once by its name, type and shape (llama.cpp loads a model twice, once without memory to fit it to the devices), and the count goes with the last buffer of the backend.
 - **A model that does not fit whole** (`XdnaPlacement::vPartial`): generating tokens then goes with the processor but the logits, and only the copies for the prompts (and those of the logits) count, so that more layers go to the NPU: 13.85 GiB of the weights of the 27B (67 %), 6.71 GiB with the processor.
 - **Measured** (`llama-bench -p 512 -n 16 -r 2 -fa 1`): a prompt of 512 tokens at 80.3 tokens per second against 36.2 with the processor alone (2.2 times); generating, 2.23 against 2.15. The same text as the processor when generating with the overlay for generating tokens. Its recurrence goes with the processor: the overlay of the prompts takes two heads of values for every head of keys, and the 27B has three.
 - **Solved on 2026/11/20: a command that did not complete (`vGemvContext`).** Before the partial mode, the budget put 8.4 GiB of the weights on the NPU with both copies; the warm-up of llama.cpp (two tokens, every layer through the chained overlay) converted their copies for generating tokens and ran 107 commands, and then the first command of the overlay of the prompts (the projections of layer 0: 4096 + 1024 inputs by 10,240 and 6,240 outputs) ended in the state `timeout` (the NPU stayed usable). On 2026/11/20 this turned out to be the exact same shared-context compute-unit issue seen in `xdna2-moe` after `Q5_K`/`Q6_K` commands: isolating `xdna2-gemv-chain` into its own hardware context `XdnaRunner::vGemvContext` (`mSubmitGemvAndWait`, see [Tuning the 13-model Q8_0 and Q6_K catalog](#tuning-the-13-model-q8_0-and-q6_k-catalog-native-q8_0-vgemvcontext-full-27b-chaining-and-prefill-overlap)) eliminated the `timeout` completely and unlocked 64/64 chained layers on Qwen3.8 27B.

### Flint 40B-A2.5B: a sliding window, attention sinks and experts

Since 2026/11/17. The fork loads `flint` (Flint 40B-A2.5B, 30.5 GiB in Q6_K): 49 layers 2048 wide, 32 heads of queries and 4 of keys and values of 128, with norms of q and k; 35 layers with a sliding window of 513 tokens and RoPE, and 14 global layers without RoPE; attention sinks (one value a head that takes part in the softmax); the first 2 layers with a dense feed-forward block of 6144 and the others with 256 experts of 512 (6 used by every token, sigmoid gating, their weights normalized) and a shared expert of 1024 without a gate; a vocabulary of 150,144, with the output apart from the embeddings.

 - **The port.** From upstream (llama.cpp 1.0.0), with what the fork had taken out: the KV cache with an interleaved sliding window (`src/llama-kv-cache-iswa.{h,cpp}`: two caches, here 14 layers with the whole context and 35 with the window; `llm_graph_input_attn_kv_iswa`, `build_attn_inp_kv_iswa` and a `build_attn` for it in `src/llama-graph.*`), its keys (`leading_dense_block_count`, `expert_shared_count`, `attention.sliding_window`, its pattern and `attention.rope_pattern`), the tensors of the sinks (`attn_sinks`, with a test of `SCALE` in `weight_buft_supported`) and of the bias of the choice of the experts (`exp_probs_b`, not in this file), and the rescaling of the activation of its experts by a power of two before their down projection (`build_amax_src1_f16`: the sums of the Q8 blocks of the processor are F16, and the activation of the experts of Flint can pass that range). The graph (`src/models/flint.cpp`) is upstream's with three changes: the RoPE with `ggml_rope_ext` and only in the layers that have it (`hparams.has_rope`; this fork has no `build_rope`), the global base and scale of the RoPE in the layers with a window (`llama_model::get_rope_freq_base`, as `get_rope_params` of upstream), and the three projections of the attention added to the graph as soon as they are made, so that the backend multiplies them in one command. Its parameters are checked with exceptions on load.
 - **The same results as upstream.** On the processor, the logits of 4,096 tokens (`llama-perplexity -c 512 -b 512 --chunks 8`) are byte for byte those of llama.cpp 1.0.0 (KL 0, the same top token in 100 %); perplexity 23.92.
 - **What goes where.** As with `qwen35moe`: the experts, their router and their SwiGLU stay with the processor, and so does generating tokens, but the logits. In a batch, the projections of the attention, the attention itself (since 2026/11/18, with its sinks: in `fXdnaGemmPackAttnP`, every row with at least one unmasked key adds exp(sink − max) for its head to its sum, while P stays scaled by the largest score of its keys; `fXdnaAttnMaskKinds` classifies the strips of the sliding-window mask as any other mask), its output with the residual sum, the 2 dense layers (their SwiGLU into the rows of `ffn_down`) and the shared expert go to the NPU.
 - **Its divergence says little.** With the NPU against the processor alone, KL 0.130 and the same top token in 85.3 % (`-c 512 -b 512 --chunks 8`, before the attention went to the NPU); with `-c 2048 -b 512 --chunks 1` on 2026/11/18, 0.1046 and 88.66 % with the attention and its sinks on the NPU against the processor alone, 0.0979 and 89.15 % with `GGML_XDNA_NO_ATTN`, and 0.0927 and 89.44 % between the two; and the processor against itself, with the variant of the CPU backend for AVX2 (`haswell`) instead of the one for AVX-512 (`zen4`), which only adds in another order, gives 0.119 and 86.3 %. Flint amplifies any difference of rounding (its sigmoid gating chooses 6 of 256 experts, and a tiny change chooses others in the layers after): with only its 2 dense layers on the NPU the KL is already 0.128. Two passes of the same processor give the same logits byte for byte. A reference in F32 (160 GB) does not fit in this laptop.
 - **Measured** (Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K): without the attention on the NPU (`llama-bench -p 512 -n 64 -r 3 -t 8`, two rounds interleaved, 2026/11/17), a prompt of 512 tokens at 223 and 226 tokens per second against 166 and 165 with the processor alone (+35 %), and generating, 19.4 and 19.0 against 18.8 and 18.4 (+3 %). With the attention and its sinks on the NPU (`llama-bench -p 512 -n 0 -d 0,2048,8192 -r 5 -t 8 -fa 1`, two rounds interleaved against `GGML_XDNA_NO_ATTN=1`, 2026/11/18): 229.5 and 236.1 against 220.9 and 206.8 with no context (+9 %), 211.1 and 210.0 against 166.7 and 159.3 with 2,048 tokens of context (+29 %), and 170.5 and 178.1 against 99.0 and 94.1 with 8,192 (+80 %, faster in every round).
 - **What is left.** On 2026/11/19 the routed experts moved to the NPU on `xdna2-moe` (next section).

### Routed experts on the NPU

Since 2026/11/19 (point 1.(a) of the pending tasks; `GGML_XDNA_NO_MOE=1` gives them back to the processor, and `GGML_XDNA_MOE_MIN_TOKENS` sets the minimum tokens a batch needs for them to run on the NPU, 1 by default). In a batch of 512 tokens, 256 experts with 6 or 8 chosen per token get only 12 to 16 rows each on average, too few for the overlay of the prompts (tiles of 64 rows, and one command per expert would pay the launch cost hundreds of times), while the chained overlay runs one matrix per phase. `xdna2-moe` (`xdna2-moe.pdi` and `.manifest`, manifest `xdna2-moe 1`, built in `_/xdna2/work/moe1` by `_/xdna2-lab/compilar_moe.sh`) multiplies up to 1,136 chunks of 1 to 24 rows by their chosen expert matrices in a single command across all 16 shim input channels and 32 cores.

 - **The overlay and its kernel** (`overlays/designs/moe_experts.py`, `moe_manifest.py`, `kernels/xdna2-moe.cc`). As in `xdna2-gemv-chain`, the memory tile splits every 9,216-byte stream object into two 4,608-byte halves for core 0 (even core row) and core 1 (odd core row), which split K in half, add their 32-bit float sums over the cascade, and emit 32-float rows of Y linearly. What changes:
   - Each core keeps up to 3 groups of 8 rows (24 rows, 27,648 bytes of `x`, moved to banks 1 and 2 so `mac_8x8_8x8T` reads `x` and `W` without bank stalls) laid out as `[tile of K][group of 8 values of K][row group][72 bytes]`: every decoded piece of weights (bfp16, Q4_K, Q5_K or Q6_K) multiplies all 1, 2 or 3 row groups in one pass with up to three 64-lane accumulators, without re-reading or re-decoding the weights of the expert.
   - Object 0 carries 16 `int32` parameters and a table of up to 1,136 chunks `(objectsX << 16) | rows` stored in the 7th object slot of `x`. When an expert has two segments (`gate_exps` and `up_exps`, `vSwiglu = 1`), core 0 stores the rows of segment 0 in `chain` (1,664 floats: up to 52 rows of 32 outputs, so up to 24 rows when an expert has 512 outputs across the 16 streams) and applies `silu(gate) * up` in place when segment 1 finishes, before emitting Y (`xdna2_moe_finish`). Core 0 uses 12,032 of its 16,384 bytes of program memory; core 1, 9,440.
 - **Weights, streams and fusions** (`xdna-moe.{h,cpp}`, `fPieceQ6KAvx2` in `xdna-gemv.cpp`, `fRunMoeStep` in `ggml-xdna.cpp`). All experts of a tensor (or a `{gate_exps, up_exps}` pair) live in one `BO_SHARE` buffer laid out as `[stream][expert][segments][objects]`, so stream `s` reads one contiguous slice of `vExpertBytes` for any chosen expert. An AVX2 path for `Q6_K` (`fPieceQ6KAvx2`) packs the 15.7 GB of 23 layers in 3.2 s (~5.0 GB/s). `fXdnaMoePlan` drives the chunks with a 3-slot ring of shim `MM2S` descriptor pairs per channel (14 of the 16 descriptors per shim column, keeping two chunks queued ahead of the array) while one linear `S2MM` transfer per stream drains all emitted rows. In the graph, `fRunMoeStep` groups `(token, slot)` pairs by expert into chunks of 1 to 24 rows, encodes each shared input row once (`fXdnaMoeEncodeRowBfp16`), fuses `{up_exps, gate_exps} + SwiGLU` into one command, transcodes the kept Y directly into the X of `down_exps` (`mRunMoeFromKept`, `fXdnaMoeTranscodeYToX`) when no processor node sits between them (`qwen35moe`), and folds the weighted reduction (`MUL` by optional `scale_s` of Flint, `MUL` by the routing weights, `VIEW`s and `ADD`s into `moe_out`) into the parallel reading of Y (`fXdnaMoeReadY`, one thread per disjoint column stream: 0 tail operations on the host).
 - **A context of its own (`XdnaRunner::vMoeContext`), and a trap of shared compute units.** Putting `xdna2-moe.pdi` into `vContext` as a third compute unit (`mConfigureContextUnits`) worked alone and with `bfp16` or `Q4_K` weights, but after a `Q5_K` or `Q6_K` MoE command followed by `512 x 2048 x 1024` on compute unit 0 (`xdna2-gemm-bfp16`), the next `512 x 512 x 2048` command on compute unit 0 (`ffn_shexp`) deterministically ended in `timeout` (the NPU stayed healthy; reproduced in 5 ms with `llama-xdna-gemv --moe --moe-switch --format q6k`). Giving `xdna2-moe.pdi` its own `XdnaContext` (`vMoeContext`, sharing the same `XdnaDevice`, `vHeap` and `BO_SHARE` buffers; 2 of the 16 hardware contexts) makes the firmware reset the partition cleanly on every switch (~1.8 to 2.0 ms) and passes in every format.
 - **Memory and zero-switch generation** (`fPlaceMoeWeights`, `fReleaseMappedExpertPages`, `fMulMat`). Whole MoE layers go to `xdna2-moe` within `GGML_XDNA_WEIGHTS_GB`, leaving headroom for the dense `bfp16` prompt copies and `output.weight` (by default, 23 of 40 layers on Qwen3.6 35B-A3B and 24 of 47 MoE layers on Flint 40B-A2.5B; 32 and 34 with `GGML_XDNA_WEIGHTS_GB=25`, safely below the ~26 GB buffer ceiling). Because a second 25 GB copy beside the mapped file would exceed the 46.7 GiB of this laptop, when `GGML_XDNA_MOE_MIN_TOKENS` is 1 `fReleaseMappedExpertPages` calls `madvise(..., MADV_DONTNEED)` on the read-only `MAP_SHARED` file pages of each converted expert tensor: `RssFile` drops by ~15.5 GiB (peak `VmRSS` 19.9 GiB on Qwen3.6 35B-A3B and 14.4 GiB on Flint 40B-A2.5B, `0.00 GiB` swap). While generating tokens, `output.weight` runs as 1 expert on `xdna2-moe` too (`fMulMat`) and `fRmsNormTaken` leaves single-row norms on the processor when `fSawExperts()` holds, so every generated token runs with 0 overlay switches (`splits` from 208 down to 48 on Qwen3.6, 296 down to 98 on Flint).
 - **Precision** (`llama-perplexity --kl-divergence`, 2026/11/19):
   - Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K (`-c 512 -b 512 --chunks 3 -fa on`, against the processor alone, PPL 9.8797): with batches of 512, PPL 9.8905, mean KL 0.00982 and the same top token in 96.34 % (0.00931 and 96.99 % without MoE on the NPU); with batches of 1 token (`-ub 1`, 106,122 `MUL_MAT_ID` projections in 70,748 commands and 0 overlay switches), PPL 9.7992, mean KL 0.00960 and 96.21 % (0.0091 and 96.6 % before).
   - Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K (`-c 2048 -b 512 --chunks 1 -fa on`, against the processor alone, PPL 18.2678): PPL 17.6989, mean KL 0.0877 and the same top token in 88.76 % (closer than 0.1046 without MoE on the NPU, and below the 0.119 between the `zen4` and `haswell` variants of the processor itself).
 - **Speed** (`llama-bench -p 512 -n 64 -r 5 -t 8 -fa 1`, two interleaved rounds after a discarded warm-up pass, 2026/11/19, tokens per second):

| Model and variant | Prompt of 512 (`pp512`), rounds 1 and 2 | Generating (`tg64`), rounds 1 and 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K, processor alone (`GGML_XDNA_DISABLE=1`) | 124.2 and 126.4 | 13.59 and 15.30 |
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K, NPU without MoE (`GGML_XDNA_NO_MOE=1`) | 194.6 and 197.5 | 15.14 and 15.18 |
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K, NPU with MoE (default, 23/40 MoE layers) | 260.1 and 258.7 (350.5 fresh) | 14.01 and 13.66 (17.28 fresh) |
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K, NPU with MoE (`GGML_XDNA_WEIGHTS_GB=25`, 32/40) | 287.5 and 358.6 | 13.58 and 15.42 |
| Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K, processor alone (`GGML_XDNA_DISABLE=1`) | 183.8 and 168.3 | 19.64 and 18.94 |
| Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K, NPU without MoE (`GGML_XDNA_NO_MOE=1`) | 270.5 and 239.1 | 20.04 and 19.61 |
| Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K, NPU with MoE (default, 24/47 MoE layers) | 293.8 and 274.7 | 18.23 and 17.30 (20.76 in `-n 32`) |
| Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K, NPU with MoE (`GGML_XDNA_WEIGHTS_GB=25`, 34/47) | 282.8 and 282.8 | 16.63 and 16.73 |

   In a prompt of 512 tokens, `xdna2-moe` speeds Qwen3.6 35B-A3B up by 32 to 81 % over the NPU without MoE (2.1 to 2.8 times the processor alone), and Flint 40B-A2.5B (only 6 active experts per token, with `build_amax_src1_f16` on the processor between `SwiGLU` and `down_exps` and two context switches per layer around `ffn_shexp`) by 9 to 15 % (1.6 times the processor alone). Generating one token (`M = 1`), each MoE layer streams 6 or 8 small slices (54 to 108 KB per channel) in 2 commands of 256 shim descriptors, so `xdna2-moe` runs within ~8 to 10 % of the processor while keeping `RssFile` ~15.5 GiB lower and swap at 0 (`GGML_XDNA_MOE_MIN_TOKENS=2` leaves single-token experts on the processor when memory allows, without dropping their file pages). Both `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-MTP-Abliterated-Q6_K.gguf` and `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-MTP-Cyber-Abliterated-Q6_K.gguf` run with 0 overlay switches.

### Dense projections and shared experts of a mixture of experts

Since 2026/11/19 (point 1.(b) of the pending tasks; `GGML_XDNA_NO_MOE_DENSE=1` turns it off to compare with `1.(a)` alone). In `1.(a)`, `fSawExperts` still left all dense projections and shared experts of 1 to 8 rows on the processor, Flint broke every MoE layer in half with its 10-node `build_amax_src1_f16` rescaling, and the default 40 % weight budget capped routed experts at 23/40 layers on Qwen3.6 and 24/47 on Flint even though `MADV_DONTNEED` dropped their mapped file pages. Because switching between `xdna2-moe` and `xdna2-gemv-chain` costs ~2.0 to 2.5 ms per switch (~160 ms per token if done every layer), every token stays on one overlay in either regime:

 - **Regime A (`GGML_XDNA_MOE_MIN_TOKENS=1`, the default): everything on `xdna2-moe`, 0 overlay switches.**
   - **Shared experts folded as virtual experts** (`fMatchMoeSharedExpert`, `fEnsureMoeWithShared`, `fRunMoeStep`). In Qwen3.6 (`ffn_shexp` of 512, equal to one routed expert, gated by `sigmoid(ffn_gate_inp_shexp · x)`) the shared expert becomes virtual expert `#256`; in Flint (`ffn_shexp` of 1024, twice one routed expert, ungated) it is sliced into virtual experts `#256` and `#257` (each `512` wide in `gate`/`up` and summing `512`-input halves in `down`). Both run inside the same two `xdna2-moe` commands as the routed experts (**0 extra NPU commands**), while `fXdnaMoeReadY` initializes each token row from `ffn_residual` (`pInitSrc`, per-stream AVX2 copy) before accumulating the weighted routed and shared slices (`0` host tail or residual operations).
   - **Pre-reading `conv_states` in `qwen35moe.cpp`** (`_/port-tools/make_qwen35moe.py`). Previously `build_moe_ffn` expanded `moe_out[il]` inside layer `il` while `build_conv_states` at the top of recurrent layer `il + 1` inserted a processor `cache_r_l` (`SCALE + GET_ROWS`) right before `ffn_gate_shexp[il]` whenever `!inp->in_place` (warm-up and `pp512`), splitting `moe_out` from `ffn_shexp` on 17 recurrent layers. Pre-building `conv_states` of layer `0` before the layer loop and of layer `il + 1` right after `attn_post_norm` (before `build_layer_ffn`), together with keying `dMoeMatrices` on both `aExps` and `aShexp`, folds 40/40 shared experts on every pass and cuts `sched_reserve` splits from `278 / 116` down to `244 / 82`.
   - **Absorbing `build_amax_src1_f16` on Flint** (`fMoeAmaxTaken`, `fMatchMoeAmax`, `fMoeAmaxOnHost`). Because `xdna2-moe` keeps its activations in `bfp16ebs8` (8-bit shared exponent with `fp32` sums, never `fp16` `Q8` block sums), the `DIV` by `2^k` followed by `MUL` by `2^k` after `down_exps` cancels out: `fRunMoeStep` absorbs all 10 `amax` nodes so Flint also uses `mRunMoeFromKept` directly from `{gate_exps, up_exps} + SwiGLU` into `down_exps`, eliminating two context switches per layer in `pp512` as well.
   - **Dense projections and leading dense FFNs on `xdna2-moe`** (`fEnsureMoeDenseGroup`, `fEnsureMoeDensePair`, `fRunDenseMoeFfn`, `fXdnaMoeEncodeRowBfp16`, `fXdnaMoeReadYTargets`). At 1 to 8 rows, multi-member attention input projections (`attn_qkv + ssm_ba` or `attn_q + attn_k + attn_v`, with fused row `RMS_NORM` and `CONCAT` redirection) and Flint's two leading dense feed-forward blocks (`blk.0`, `blk.1`, guarded in `fFeedForward` so they do not switch to `xdna2-gemv-chain`) run as 1-expert commands on `xdna2-moe`. `ssm_out` and `attn_output` (`2048` outputs) stay on the processor by default (`GGML_XDNA_MOE_ATTN_OUT=0`: measured 17.13 vs 17.03 t/s on Qwen3.6 and 18.57 vs 18.05 t/s on Flint in `-n 64 -r 3`).
 - **Regime B (`GGML_XDNA_MOE_MIN_TOKENS` ≥ 2 or `GGML_XDNA_NO_MOE=1`): `fSharedExpertChain` on `xdna2-gemv-chain`, 0 switches while generating.** When single-token routed experts run on the processor, `fSharedExpertChain` chains `{gate_shexp, up_shexp} + SwiGLU + down_shexp + optional sigmoid gate + ADD(moe_out) + ADD(ffn_residual)` together with `attn_norm` and `attn_qkv`/`wqkv` (`+ CONCAT`) of layer `il + 1` (or `output_norm + output.weight` in the last layer) in one 2- or 3-phase command, folding the scalar shared-expert gate `g` by pre-scaling `(moe_out + residual) / g` into phase 1's `cAdd` epilogue and scaling phase 2's `w_norm * g` and `eps / g^2`. Removing `vPhase.vEps` from `fXdnaChainKey` (`fXdnaChainPlan` never reads `vEps`; `fXdnaChainWriteParameters` writes it per command) keeps instruction buffers cached instead of exhausting the 64 MiB `DEV_HEAP` after ~42 tokens (`XdnaDevice::mIoctl` also caps `EAGAIN` retries at 64 so a full `DEV_HEAP` fails cleanly instead of spinning). When `GGML_XDNA_MOE_MIN_TOKENS` > 1, `MADV_DONTNEED` cannot drop the mapped expert pages because the processor still reads them at `M = 1`, so `fPlaceMoeWeights` counts both copies (`2 * pBytesW`), keeping 12 layers on `xdna2-moe` for `pp512` with `0.00 GiB` swap.
 - **Automatic 100 % MoE placement with `MADV_DONTNEED`** (`fProbe`, `fPlaceWeights`, `fPlaceMoeWeights`). When `GGML_XDNA_WEIGHTS_GB` is not set, `GGML_XDNA_MOE_MIN_TOKENS` is 1 and `GGML_XDNA_COPY_WEIGHTS` is unset, each converted 3D expert layer immediately releases its read-only `MAP_SHARED` file pages (`fReleaseMappedExpertPages`), replacing `RssFile` one-for-one instead of adding on top of it. Keeping the 40 % limit (`vWeightsLimit`, 20.1 GB here) on net extra 2D copies (preserving partial mode on dense models such as Qwen3.8 27B) while allowing up to 76 % of `MemTotal` (`vMoeWeightsLimit`, 38.1 GB here, computed live from `sysconf(_SC_PHYS_PAGES)`) across total NPU `SHARE` buffers places **40/40 (100 %) MoE layers of Qwen3.6 35B-A3B** (~28.8 GB of `SHARE` buffers, `RssFile` last `0.01 GiB`) and **47/47 (100 %) MoE layers + 2 dense layers of Flint 40B-A2.5B** (~33.7 GB of `SHARE` buffers, `RssFile` last `0.01 GiB`) on the NPU.
 - **Precision at 100 % MoE placement** (`llama-perplexity -f texto.txt -c 512 --chunks 1 -fa on --kl-divergence` against `GGML_XDNA_DISABLE=1`, 2026/11/19):
   - Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K (40/40 layers, CPU PPL 10.8463): `-b 512` PPL 10.5953, mean KL **0.01402 ± 0.00239** (same top token 95.69 %); `-b 1 -ub 1` (40,528 MoE commands + 20,777 dense MoE commands, 20,264 shared experts folded, 0 overlay switches) PPL 10.7769, mean KL **0.01356 ± 0.00234** (same top token **97.65 %**).
   - Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K (47/47 MoE + 2 dense layers, 33.7 GB `SHARE`, CPU PPL 27.7394): `-b 512` PPL 27.1587, mean KL **0.08918 ± 0.04650** (same top token 86.67 %); `-b 1 -ub 1` (47,710 MoE commands + 28,472 dense MoE commands, 23,855 shared experts folded, 0 overlay switches) PPL 27.0030, mean KL **0.05470 ± 0.01241** (same top token **88.24 %**, less than half the 0.119 between `zen4` and `haswell` on the processor itself).
 - **Speed and memory** (`llama-bench -p 512 -n 64 -r 5 -t 8 -fa 1`, two interleaved rounds after a discarded warm-up pass, plus `_/xdna2-lab/medir_memoria.py`, 2026/11/19, tokens per second):

| Model and variant | Prompt of 512 (`pp512`), rounds 1 and 2 | Generating (`tg64`), rounds 1 and 2 | Memory (`medir_memoria.py`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K, NPU default (40/40 MoE, Regime A) | **394.87 and 392.16** | **16.93 and 16.97** (17.09–18.29 in `-n 64`) | `RssFile` last `0.01 GiB`, `VmSwap` max `0.14 GiB` |
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K, `GGML_XDNA_MOE_MIN_TOKENS=2` (Regime B) | 310.19 and 305.86 | 14.71 and 14.70 | `RssFile` last `8.45 GiB`, `VmSwap` max `0.00 GiB` |
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K, `GGML_XDNA_NO_MOE_DENSE=1` (`1.(a)` only, 40/40) | 388.18 and 392.47 | 14.15 and 14.17 | — |
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K, processor alone (`GGML_XDNA_DISABLE=1`) | 181.26 and 182.74 | 15.80 and 15.66 | — |
| Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K, NPU default (47/47 MoE, Regime A) | **258.85 and 323.67** | **14.84 and 16.79** (17.51–18.57 in `-n 64`) | `RssFile` last `0.01 GiB`, `VmSwap` max `0.08 GiB` |
| Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K, `GGML_XDNA_MOE_MIN_TOKENS=2` (Regime B) | 257.14 and 258.65 | 16.51 and 16.91 (20.47 fresh in `-n 64`) | `RssFile` last `8.29 GiB`, `VmSwap` max `0.00 GiB` |
| Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K, `GGML_XDNA_NO_MOE_DENSE=1` (`1.(a)` only, 47/47) | 313.79 and 318.25 | 15.35 and 15.71 | — |
| Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K, processor alone (`GGML_XDNA_DISABLE=1`) | 169.13 and 167.84 | 19.05 and 18.79 | — |

   On Qwen3.6 35B-A3B (8 routed + 1 virtual expert across 40 layers), folding all 40 shared experts and running dense input projections on `xdna2-moe` raises `pp512` to **392–395 t/s** (**2.16×** the processor alone) and `tg64` by **+19.8 %** over `1.(a)` alone (**16.93–16.97 t/s vs 15.66–15.80 t/s on the processor alone: +7 to +8 % faster than the processor even in single-token generation**). On Flint 40B-A2.5B (only 6 routed + 2 virtual experts across 47 layers, 94 shim-ring commands per token), absorbing `build_amax_src1_f16` and placing 47/47 layers lifts steady-state `pp512` to **323.7 t/s** (**1.93×** the processor alone) and steady-state `tg64` to **16.79–16.91 t/s** (+7 % over `1.(a)` alone, within ~10 % of the processor's 18.8–19.1 t/s while dropping `RssFile` from 30.5 GiB to `0.01 GiB`).

### Speech synthesis: Qwen3-TTS

Since 2026/11/19. The fork loads all three families of `qwen3tts` — `Qwen3-TTS-12Hz-0.6B-Base`, `1.7B-Base`, `0.6B-CustomVoice`, `1.7B-CustomVoice` and `1.7B-VoiceDesign`, each tested in `Q4_K_M`, `Q5_K_M`, `Q6_K`, `Q8_0` and `BF16` paired with `mmproj-Qwen3-TTS-12Hz-*-{Q8_0,BF16}.gguf` (`CustomVoice` and `VoiceDesign` converted from the official `Qwen/Qwen3-TTS-12Hz-*` checkpoints by `_/port-tools/convert_qwen3tts_to_gguf.py` plus `llama-quantize`; after benchmarking, the 10 `BF16` files were deleted at the user's request since XDNA2 converts both `BF16` and `Q8_0` to `bfp16`, leaving 25 GGUF files in `~/IA/Modelos/`) — and builds `llama-tts` (`tools/tts/tts.cpp`) and a Qwen3-TTS-only `libmtmd` (`tools/mtmd/`).

 - **Architecture and where the work goes.** Every 12.5 Hz frame (80 ms of 24 kHz audio) runs two autoregressive transformers before the convolutional vocoder:
   1. **Talker** (`src/models/qwen3tts.cpp`, `LLM_ARCH_QWEN3TTS`): 28 dense GQA layers (`1024` wide with `n_ff = 3072` on `0.6B`; `2048` wide with `n_ff = 6144` on `1.7B`), QK RMS norms, interleaved M-RoPE (`LLAMA_ROPE_TYPE_IMROPE`, `[24, 20, 20]`), and `n_vocab = 151,936` (`tok_embd`) with only `3,072` active codec logits (`output.weight`, padded to `n_vocab` with `-INFINITY`). Expanding `{Qcur, Kcur, Vcur}` and `output` immediately lets `fFeedForward` chain all 28 layers plus `output_norm + output.weight` on `xdna2-gemv-chain` (`29` commands per frame).
   2. **`code_gen` multi-codebook predictor** (`tools/mtmd/models/qwen3tts-gen.cpp`, inside `mmproj`): for *every* Talker frame, a 5-layer `1024`-wide transformer (`n_ff = 3072`) runs **16 single-token autoregressive steps** (**80 transformer layer evaluations** plus **15 `[1024, 2048]` codebook heads** per frame) to predict RVQ codebooks `1..15`. In upstream, `a.gen.code.head.weight` is a 3D `[1024, 2048, 15]` tensor accessed via `ggml_view_2d`, which `fMulMatTaken` leaves on the processor (views are not standalone weight tensors). Pre-splitting it at load time into 15 standalone 2D `GGML_OP_NONE` tensors (`gen_code_head_2d[0..14]`) and calling `ggml_build_forward_order` on `{q, k, v}` lets `fFeedForward` chain **78 of the 79 FFN blocks per frame (98.7 %)** — including layer 4's `output_norm` + codebook head — on `xdna2-gemv-chain` with **0 overlay switches** between Talker and `code_gen`.
   3. **`code2wav` causal DAC vocoder** (`tools/mtmd/models/qwen3tts-gen.cpp`, inside `mmproj`): every `72` frames (`5.76 s` of audio, `wav_tfm_swa = 72`), an 8-layer sliding-window pre-transformer (`N = 72`), 2 ConvNeXt blocks (`T = 144, 288`), 6 transposed upsampling convolutions (`strides 2, 2, 4, 5, 8`, `T` up to `138,240`) and 25 causal dilated SnakeBeta residual convolutions synthesize 24 kHz PCM (`~300 GFLOP` per 72-frame chunk). `ggml_conv_1d` and `ggml_conv_transpose_1d` wrap `IM2COL + MUL_MAT` over reshaped 3D weight views; precomputing standalone 2D `F32` `GGML_OP_NONE` weight tensors (`conv1d_w_2d` `[K*IC, OC]` for `OC > 1` and `conv1d_tr_w_2d` `[IC, K*OC]`) when `XDNA` is active (`use_conv_2d`) sends all 25 multi-channel causal convolutions and all 6 transposed convolutions — alongside the pre-transformer and ConvNeXt `pw1`/`pw2` — to `xdna2-gemm-bfp16`, paying **only 1 overlay switch** (`~2.5 ms`) for the entire utterance.
   4. **`ECAPA-TDNN` speaker encoder and conditioning modes** (`tools/mtmd/models/qwen3tts-spkenc.cpp`, `tools/mtmd/mtmd.cpp`, `common/arg.cpp`):
      - **`Base` (zero-shot voice cloning, `--tts-speaker-file <ref.wav>`)**: `miniaudio` decodes `.wav`/`.mp3`/`.flac`, Slaney mel features feed `ECAPA-TDNN` once in exact `F32` on the processor (~15 ms; sending it to `XDNA` added 8 overlay switches around `T = 1` squeeze-excitation projections and rounded `speaker_embd`), and the resulting speaker vector is injected into the codec header.
      - **`CustomVoice` (9 built-in speakers + optional style instruction on `1.7B`, `--tts-voice NAME [--tts-instruct TEXT]`)**: `mtmd_context` discovers every `<|spk_*|>` token in `text_model` (`aiden`, `dylan`, `eric`, `ono_anna`, `ryan`, `serena`, `sohee`, `uncle_fu`, `vivian`), exposes them via `mtmd_gen_audio_get_info` / `mtmd_gen_audio_voice_name` / case-insensitive `mtmd_gen_audio_get_voice`, and injects `sum_vec(tts_pad, row(<|spk_name|>))` in the codec header (their `mmproj` omits `ECAPA-TDNN`). When `--tts-lang` is omitted (`auto`, using `<|codec_nothink|>`) or `chinese`, `eric` and `dylan` automatically select `sichuan_dialect` and `beijing_dialect`, matching Qwen's reference `qwen3_tts_model.py`.
      - **`VoiceDesign` (`1.7B`, creating new voices from text descriptions, `--tts-instruct TEXT`)**: prepends `<|im_start|>user\n{instruct}<|im_end|>\n` text embeddings (`row(t)`) before `<|im_start|>assistant\n` (`1.7B-CustomVoice` uses the exact same prefix for emotion/prosody/speed control, while `0.6B` warns and ignores `--tts-instruct`). `mtmd_select_device` (`tools/mtmd/mtmd.cpp`) selects `GGML_BACKEND_DEVICE_TYPE_ACCEL` (`XDNA`) when no GPU backend is registered, and `mtmd_subcontext::warmup` checks CPU `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` support when the device is an accelerator so 1-token flash attention stays enabled.
 - **Precision and audio quality** (measured on 2026/11/19):
   - **Talker CPU parity vs upstream `llama.cpp 1.0.0`** (`Qwen3-TTS-12Hz-0.6B-Base-BF16.gguf`, `llama-perplexity --kl-divergence`, `-c 512 -b 512 --chunks 1`): **mean KLD `-0.000000 ± 0.000001`**, maximum KLD `0.000039`, **same top token `100.000 %`**.
   - **End-to-end CPU parity vs upstream `llama.cpp 1.0.0`** (`0.6B BF16 + mmproj-BF16`, `-n 1 --temp 0 --seed 42`, full pipeline `miniaudio` → Slaney mel → `ECAPA-TDNN` → Talker → 16-step `code_gen` → `code2wav`): **waveform Pearson correlation `0.999997`**, 20 ms envelope correlation **`1.000000`**, MAE `0.000015` (longer greedy runs diverge only at discrete RVQ near-ties because of the fork's 8-row vectorized `RMS_NORM` in `ggml-cpu/ops.cpp`).
   - **NPU `code2wav` (`bfp16` GEMM) vs CPU `F32` `code2wav` on identical RVQ codes** (`0.6B Q8_0`, `-n 16`, `30,720` samples): **waveform correlation `0.999798`**, 20 ms envelope correlation **`0.999977`**, MAE `0.001467`, cutting vocoder time by **38 to 43 %** (`2.03 s` down to `1.00–1.42 s` for 72 frames) with `1` overlay switch total.
 - **Pure Talker speed — `Base`** (`llama-bench -p 512 -n 64 -r 5 -t 8 -fa 1`, interleaved NPU vs `GGML_XDNA_DISABLE=1` after a discarded warm-up pass, 2026/11/19, tokens per second):

   In `0.6B Q4_K_M`, `tok_embd` takes ~160 MiB of the 345 MiB file so the 28 active layers weigh only ~180 MiB, small enough for the processor's L3 cache to help while the NPU pays the fixed launch cost of 29 commands per token; from `0.6B Q8_0` up and across **every** `1.7B` format (`1.24×` to `2.54×` in `tg64`, `4.14×` to `9.30×` in `pp512`), the NPU's ~70 GB/s DMA wins, and unquantized `BF16` runs at the exact same speed as `Q8_0` on XDNA2 because both convert once to 1.125-byte `bfp16`.

 - **End-to-end `llama-tts` synthesis — `CustomVoice` and `VoiceDesign`** (`-n 72` frames = `5.76 s` of 24 kHz audio, `-t 8 -c 2048 --temp 0.9 --seed 42`, `--tts-voice serena` + `--tts-instruct` on `1.7B-CustomVoice` and `--tts-instruct` persona prompt on `1.7B-VoiceDesign`, median of 3 interleaved NPU vs processor rounds; `{Q4_K_M..Q8_0}` paired with `mmproj-Q8_0`, `BF16` paired with `mmproj-BF16`). Because step 72 flushes the 72-frame `code2wav` window inside `total_s - prompt_s`, "Post-prompt synth+voc" below is the combined steady-state synthesis + vocoder rate (`72 / (total_s - prompt_s)`). In a cold one-shot CLI run whose prompt reaches `GGML_XDNA_MIN_BATCH` (`>= 40` tokens when `--tts-instruct` is present), `prompt_s` on the NPU (`~0.34 s` on `0.6B`, `~1.0 s` on `1.7B`) includes the one-time lazy `bfp16` conversion of the Talker prompt weights (paid once at startup in a persistent server, or skipped in one-shot CLI with `GGML_XDNA_MIN_BATCH=128`, where `prompt_s` drops to `0.08–0.14 s` and `1.7B-CustomVoice Q4_K_M` synthesizes `5.44 s` of speech in `5.05 s` = `1.08×` real-time). Even with that cold conversion included, **the NPU beats the processor in all 15 models**:

 - **Voice creation, style control and usage notes**:
   - **Voice creation from text (`1.7B-VoiceDesign-Q8_0`, `--tts-instruct`, zero audio files)**: describing a *50-year-old deep male documentary narrator* synthesized `7.44 s` (`93` frames, `-21.3 dBFS`, `73.9 %` active speech, `0` non-finite samples) at **`F0 = 81.9 Hz`**, whereas describing a *20s bright cheerful young woman* on the exact same Spanish sentence synthesized `6.24 s` (`78` frames, `-19.5 dBFS`, `86.5 %` active) at **`F0 = 333.3 Hz`** (**4.07× pitch shift** controlled purely by text), and an *epic fantasy narrator* in English yielded **`F0 = 89.2 Hz`** (`6.16 s`, `-25.8 dBFS`).
   - **`VoiceDesign` → `Base` two-stage workflow**: feeding the `81.9 Hz` WAV created by `1.7B-VoiceDesign` into `0.6B-Base-Q4_K_M` via `--tts-speaker-file` cloned that synthetic persona at **`1.17×` real-time** (`8.00 s` generated in `6.82 s`) while preserving **`F0 = 86.5 Hz`** (`-20.2 dBFS`, `0` non-finite samples).
   - **`CustomVoice` speakers and dialects (`1.7B-CustomVoice-Q4_K_M`)**: verified `serena` (`240.0 Hz`, `es`), `ryan` (`242.4 Hz`, excited `en`, `5.44 s` in `5.05 s` = `1.08×` RTF), `aiden` (`128.3 Hz`, `es`), `eric` (`130.1 Hz`, automatic `sichuan_dialect`) and `dylan` (`159.5 Hz`, automatic `beijing_dialect`), plus natural EOS stopping across all 25 models (`Base`, `CustomVoice` and `VoiceDesign`: `51–78` frames = `4.08–6.24 s`, `0` non-finite samples, up to **`1.20×` E2E RTF** on `0.6B-CustomVoice`).
   - **Sampling and context**: `Base` checkpoints **require** `--tts-speaker-file <ref.wav>`; `CustomVoice` checkpoints **require** `--tts-voice <name>`; all Qwen3-TTS checkpoints expect stochastic sampling (`--temp 0.9 --top-k 50`, written into GGUF metadata by `convert_qwen3tts_to_gguf.py`), and `-c 2048` shrinks the KV cache from `3,584 MiB` (`n_ctx_train = 32,768`) to `224 MiB` (`0.6B`) / `448 MiB` (`1.7B`).

### Tuning the 13-model Q8_0 and Q6_K catalog: native Q8_0, vGemvContext, full 27B chaining and prefill overlap

Since 2026/11/20. The user's model library in `/home/nipegun/IA/Modelos/GGUF/` was reorganized into 13 models (18 `.gguf` files, ~108.6 GiB) exclusively in `Q8_0` and `Q6_K`: six dense models (`Qwen3.5` `0.8B`, `2B`, `4B` and `9B` in `Q8_0`, `Granite 4.2 3B` in `Q8_0`, and `Qwen3.8 27B` in `Q6_K`), two mixtures of experts (`Qwen3.6 35B-A3B` and `Flint 40B-A2.5B` in `Q6_K`) and five `Qwen3-TTS 12Hz` speech-synthesis models (`Base` `0.6B`/`1.7B`, `CustomVoice` `0.6B`/`1.7B` and `VoiceDesign` `1.7B`, all `Q8_0` + `mmproj-Q8_0`). Four structural bottlenecks held several of those models back from the NPU's ~70 GB/s bandwidth ceiling in generation and its ~26–28 TOPS `bfp16` GEMM ceiling in prefill:

 1. **Granite 4.2 3B chaining and unit-scale pruning (`src/models/granite.cpp`)**:
    - `granite.cpp` built `build_qkv` without expanding `{Qcur, Kcur, Vcur}` immediately and emitted `ggml_scale(ctx0, cur, f_residual_scale)` and `ggml_scale(ctx0, cur, 1.0f / f_logit_scale)` even when both scales equal `1.0f` (`Granite 4.2 3B`). Those `GGML_OP_SCALE(1.0)` nodes split `fFeedForward` on all 40 layers (~201 single-matrix commands per token instead of 41) and blocked `RMS_NORM` and residual `ADD` fusions in prompts.
    - Expanding `{Qcur, Kcur, Vcur}` right after `build_qkv` and skipping `ggml_scale` when the scale is `1.0f` (marked `llama.cpp-xdna`) chained **40/40 layers** (`41` commands/token), raising `Granite 4.2 3B Q8_0` generation from `14.49 t/s` to **`18.53 t/s` (+27.9 %, `68.4 GB/s` = 97.7 % of the 70 GB/s NPU ceiling)** and `pp512` to **`1,367.3 t/s` (11.82× CPU)**.
 2. **Isolated `vGemvContext`, dense `MADV_DONTNEED`, L2-resident packing + AVX2+F16C dequantizers, `cChain = 1,728` and full 27B placement (`xdna-runner.{h,cpp}`, `xdna-chain.cpp`, `xdna-gemm.{h,cpp}`, `ggml-xdna.cpp`)**:
    - **Why 27B timed out on 2026/11/17**: `xdna2-gemv-chain` shared `vContext` with `xdna2-gemm-bfp16` as compute unit 1, triggering the exact same firmware timeout on `xdna2-gemm-bfp16` after `Q6_K` commands as `xdna2-moe` had before `vMoeContext`. Moving `xdna2-gemv-chain` into its own `XdnaContext` (`vGemvContext`, `vGemvCommand`, `mSubmitGemvAndWait`, 3 of 16 hardware contexts in total) eliminated the timeout permanently.
    - **Two-tier dense placement and `MADV_DONTNEED` (`fNoteDenseCached`, `fPlaceWeights`)**: once a `blk.*` 2D tensor has both its prompt and generation NPU copies, `fNoteDenseCached` calls `madvise(..., MADV_DONTNEED)` on its read-only `MAP_SHARED` file pages. When neither `GGML_XDNA_WEIGHTS_GB` nor `GGML_XDNA_COPY_WEIGHTS` is set, models up to `0.48 * MemTotal` (`24.1 GB`, including `Qwen3.5 9B Q8_0`) keep both copies resident, while larger dense models up to `0.68 * MemTotal` (`34.1 GB`, `Qwen3.8 27B Q6_K`) keep **100 % of their 64 layers resident in `xdna2-gemv-chain`** (`22.4 GB` of `Q6_K` buffers) and stream prompt weights through two reusable `bfp16` buffers (`vStreamPrompt`, `aStreamMatrices[0..1]`, `pReuseBuffer = true`).
    - **`cChain = 1,728` floats (`overlays/designs/gemv_chain.py`, `xdna-chain.cpp`)**: `Qwen3.8 27B` has `n_ff = 17,408 = 16 * 1,088`, 64 floats per stream above the previous `vGateMax = 1,024` (`cChain = 1,664`). Expanding `cChain` on core 0 from `1,664` to `1,728` floats (`320` residual + `320` `h` + `1,088` gate, `+256` bytes in data memory) unlocked **64/64 chained FFN + next-layer commands** (`65` commands/token vs `257`), lifting `27B` `tg64` from `2.23 t/s` to **`3.02 ± 0.04 t/s` (+35.4 %, `68.9 GB/s` = 98.4 % of 70 GB/s)**.
    - **L2-resident strip packing and AVX2+F16C dequantization (`fXdnaGemmPackBRows`, `fDequantizeQ8_0Avx2`, `fDequantizeQ6KAvx2`)**: because `-DGGML_NATIVE=OFF` compiles `libggml-base.so` for baseline x86-64 without F16C or AVX2, `ggml_get_type_traits(type)->to_float` ran scalar C loops (`~0.8 GB/s`) into full-matrix `aFloats` buffers up to `356 MB`. Dequantizing 64-output strips directly into L2-resident scratch (`1.28 MB` per thread) with `__attribute__((target("avx2,f16c")))` cut `27B` load time from `42.5 s` to **`15.1 s`** and raised `27B` streamed `pp512` from `80.3 t/s` to **`115.70 ± 2.35 t/s` (3.20× CPU, +44.1 %)**.
 3. **Generalized `1:1..4:1` prompt recurrence, half-split `SwiGLU` with early `ffn_down` launch, fused attention gate and parallel cache flush (`xdna-gdnp.cpp`, `xdna-gemm.cpp`, `xdna-runner.cpp`, `src/llama-context.cpp`, `ggml-xdna.cpp`)**:
    - `fXdnaGdnShape`, `fXdnaGdnPack`, `fXdnaGdnUnpack` and `fGdnTaken` now accept any integer head ratio `1 ≤ vValueHeads / vKeyHeads ≤ 4` instead of hardcoding `2:1`, moving all `1:1` recurrences of `Qwen3.5 0.8B` and `2B` (`18/18` layers) and all `3:1` recurrences of `Qwen3.8 27B` (`48/48` layers) onto the NPU.
    - `fRangesForOverlap` splits `{ffn_gate, ffn_up}` into two `256`-aligned halves per matrix whenever `vHalf >= 1,024 && vHalf <= pLargest` (enabling fused `SwiGLU` + `fPackSwiglu` on `0.8B`, `2B`, `TTS-0.6B`, `TTS-1.7B` and `27B`), and `fMulMatGlu` submits piece 0 of `ffn_down` (`vEarlySent`) as soon as half 0 finishes so piece 0 runs on the NPU while the host unpacks half 1.
    - `llama_context::graph_get_cb` assigns full-attention `gate_sigmoid` + `attn_gated` to XDNA (`fAttnGateStep`, fusing `SIGMOID + MUL` in AVX2 and saving 16–32 scheduler splits per pass), `xdna-gemm.cpp` vectorizes 16-wide `bf16 -> f32` strip unpacking, and `mFlushShared` parallelizes `clflushopt` across worker threads only above `512 KB` (`256 KB` in `mFlushMoeY`, avoiding thread-wakeup overhead on `73–82 KB` `M = 1` MoE buffers). On `Qwen3.5 0.8B Q8_0`, `pp512` jumped from `2,406.0 t/s` to **`3,900.9 t/s` (+62.1 %)**.
 4. **Native `Q8_0` (`XdnaGemvFormat::cQ8_0`) in `xdna2-gemv-chain` and `xdna2-moe`, plus cached 1-expert MoE plans (`kernels/xdna2-gemv-chain.cc`, `kernels/xdna2-moe.cc`, `xdna-gemv.{h,cpp}`, `xdna-insts.{h,cpp}`, `xdna-moe.cpp`, `xdna-runner.{h,cpp}`)**:
    - Ten of the 13 catalog models use `Q8_0`, which previously expanded to `bfp16` (`2,304` bytes per 2,048-weight piece = `1.125` bytes/weight). `XdnaGemvFormat::cQ8_0 = 4` (`"q8_0"`) packs 8 `block_q8_0` blocks per 256-input sub-block as 16 `bf16` scales (32-byte aligned, loaded in pairs via `aie::load_v<16>` and `aie::shuffle_down(vPairD, 8)` to respect AIE2P's 32-byte vector load alignment) followed by 256 raw two's-complement `int8` bytes reshaped `[8 outputs × 32 K]` (`272` bytes/sub-block, **`2,176` bytes/piece = `1.0625` bytes/weight**, 5.6 % less DMA traffic and zero `bfp16` conversion, decoded without host bias via `vC = aie::select(v128, v256, aie::ge(vU, uint8(128)))` and `aie::msc`).
    - Keeping `fStageWords<3>` fully unrolled for `M = 1` while compacting multi-row `fStageWords<5,9,18>` into one loop shrank `fPiece` in `xdna2-gemv-chain.cc` by `688` bytes (`3,168 -> 2,480` B; note that `pragma clang loop unroll(disable)` on `vGroup = 0..3` *increased* Peano `-Os` code size by `336` B, and `-Os` on `xdna2_chain_finish` hit a Peano `G_STORE (s256)` `InstructionSelect` crash, so `FINISH` stays `-O2`), fitting all five formats (`bfp16`, `q4k`, `q5k`, `q6k`, `q8_0`) within the 16 KiB program memory of all 32 cores in both `work/chain9-q8` (`xdna2-gemv 9`) and `work/moe1-q8` (`xdna2-moe 1`). Single-pass `llama-xdna-gemv` checks passed with `0` error against `ggml` `Q8_0` (`66.8 GB/s` sustained on `1 × 4096 × 12288 q8_0`).
    - Caching 1-expert dense MoE instruction streams (`aMoeDensePlans`) and writing multi-expert streams into reusable runner buffers (`XdnaInstructions::mFinishInto`, `aMoeInstScratch`, `aMoeEncodedScratch`) raised `Qwen3.6 35B-A3B Q6_K` `tg64` to **`18.02 ± 0.10 t/s` (`1.15×` CPU)** and pushed **`Flint 40B-A2.5B Q6_K` `tg64` to `19.12 ± 0.08 t/s` (`1.01×` CPU, up from `16.79 t/s` = +13.9 %)**, so **every model in the catalog now beats the processor alone in both prefill and generation**.

 - **Complete 13-model catalog benchmark** (`llama-bench -p 512 -n 64 -r 3 --delay 3 -t 8 -fa 1` and `llama-tts -n 72 -t 8 -c 2048 --temp 0.9 --seed 42`, measured on 2026/11/20 on AC power with zero background load):

### Qwen3.8 Flash Next (qwen4exp, 512x56B): CPU port, native Q5_1, partial-tile K/N compaction and >RAM MGLRU / USB behavior

Since 2026/11/21. Lowering the BIOS integrated-GPU VRAM reservation raised host RAM seen by Linux from `46.7 GiB` (`48.97 GB`) to **`61.47 GiB` (`64,455,228 kB` = `66.00 GB`)** in order to run `Qwen3.8 Flash Next` (`general.architecture = qwen4exp`, *"A Preview of the Qwen4 Architecture"*, `512x56B` / `179.55 B` parameters, `1,256` tensors) in both `MinMem113.5GB-Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-MTP-Abliterated-Q4_K_XL.gguf` (`105.44 GiB` on local LUKS NVMe) and `MinMem191.3GB-Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-MTP-Abliterated-Q8_0.gguf` (`177.87 GiB` on external USB 3.2 Gen 1 `5 Gbps` storage):

 1. **Architecture geometry, CPU port and byte-for-byte parity (`src/models/qwen4exp.cpp`, `src/llama-memory-hybrid-idx.{h,cpp}`, `src/llama-arch.{h,cpp}`, `src/llama-hparams.h`, `src/llama-model.cpp`)**:
    - `qwen4exp` combines `48` trunk layers (`blk.0..47`, plus `1` skipped `blk.48` MTP layer) in a `3:1` hybrid of `36` Gated Delta Net linear-attention layers (`ssm.group_count = 16`, `ssm.time_step_rank = 48`, `ssm.state_size = 128`, `ssm.inner_size = 6,144` -> `3:1` head ratio, gated by `SIGMOID` instead of `SILU`) and `12` block-sparse **QSA** full-attention layers (`24` query heads, `2` KV heads, `key/value_length = 256`, `indexer.head_count = 4`, `indexer.key_length = 128`, `indexer.top_k = 2,048`, `compress_ratio = 4`, backed by `llama_memory_hybrid_idx` plus fused `lightning_indexer` and `top_k_mask`), wrapped in **4-stream Hyper-Connections** (`dsv4_hc_mult = 4`, `hc_low_rank = 320`, `10,240`-wide residual stream with fused `dsv4_hc_pre`/`post`), **lazy Per-Layer n-gram Hash Token Embeddings (PLE)** on `blk.1` (`per_layer_token_embd.weight` `[160, 320,001,536]`, marked `TENSOR_READ_LAZY`: `26.82 GiB` `IQ4_NL` in `Q4_K_XL` and `50.66 GiB` `Q8_0` in `Q8_0`, reading only `16` rows ≈ `1.4–2.7 KiB/token` on demand), and **`512` routed experts of hidden width `n_ff_exp = 640`** (`10` active + `1` sigmoid-gated shared expert of width `640` per token).
    - All divisors, array lengths, M-RoPE sections, SSM head dimensions and PLE head ranges are validated on load with `std::runtime_error`, and `{ple_key, ple_value}` as well as `{wq, wk, wv, index_q_proj, index_k_proj}` expand together so `fMulMat` batches them into single NPU commands.
    - **Byte-for-byte CPU parity** against upstream `llama.cpp 1.0.0` (`_/upstream/build-vulkan/bin/llama-perplexity -dev none -ngl 0 -c 128 -b 128 --chunks 1`): `PPL = 26.3407 ± 2.9102` vs `26.3407 ± 2.9102`, **`KL = -0.00000 ± 0.00000` (`Same top p = 100.000 %`)**.
 2. **Zero-copy `mmap` protection and oversized USB readahead mitigation (`src/llama-model.cpp`, `ggml/src/ggml-cpu/ggml-cpu.c`)**:
    - When `ml.use_mmap && ml.n_bytes > (cpu_total / 4) * 3`, `llama_model_base::load_tensors` automatically disables `use_extra_bufts` (`CPU_REPACK`), eliminating `34.2 GiB` of anonymous heap weight duplication on `Q4_K_XL` (`0` swap) and preventing immediate OOM on `177.9 GiB` `Q8_0`.
    - On external USB UAS bridges (`Realtek RTL9210B`, `/dev/sda`), Linux sets `read_ahead_kb = 65,532 KiB` (`64 MiB`), which `init_mappings`'s `POSIX_FADV_SEQUENTIAL` doubled to `128 MiB`: every single `1.66 MiB` expert fault on CPU fallback layers pulled `38–77` unused experts (~`14 GB/token` across `32` layers -> `32.4 s/token` = `0.03 t/s`!). `llama_fd_read_ahead_kb` inspects `/sys/dev/block/<maj>:<min>/[../]queue/read_ahead_kb` and `/sys/class/bdi/`: only when `read_ahead_kb > 2,048 KiB` (preserving native `128 KiB` NVMe/LUKS streaming untouched), it skips whole-file `POSIX_MADV_WILLNEED`, marks CPU-mapped 3D expert tensors `POSIX_MADV_RANDOM`, and enables `GGML_CPU_MMAP_EXPERT_PREFETCH=1` in `ggml_compute_forward_mul_mat_id` (prefetching only uncached active `1.66 MiB` experts via `mincore` + `MADV_WILLNEED` in sparse `tg` while switching to `MADV_SEQUENTIAL` in dense `pp`). On `Q8_0` over USB `5 Gbps`, token generation jumped **`40×` on short prompts (`0.03 -> 1.21 t/s`) and `19×` on sustained `tg64` (`0.03 -> 0.58 t/s`)**.
 3. **Host fusions and AVX2+F16C dequantizers (`ggml-xdna.cpp`, `xdna-glu.{h,cpp}`, `xdna-gemm.{h,cpp}`, `xdna-runner.{h,cpp}`)**:
    - Added `fXdnaSigmoidMul` (AVX2 + scalar fallback) and `pSigmoid` plumbing through `fXdnaGemmPackGatedNorm` / `mPackGatedNormRows`, plus traversal of zero-offset contiguous `GGML_OP_VIEW`s (`z_2d`) in `fThroughReshapes`, fusing `qwen4exp`'s `SIGMOID` gated linear-attention norm directly into `ssm_out` (`pp512` scheduler splits `847 -> 738`).
    - Added `__attribute__((target("avx2,f16c")))` row dequantizers `fDequantizeQ5_1Avx2` and `fDequantizeBf16Avx2` for fast `bfp16` packing (`indexer.{q,k}_proj` are `BF16`).
 4. **Overlay and kernel compaction: native `cQ5_1`, `cParamTailGroups` and symmetric `cParamTailSubs` (`work/moe1-q51-tail`: `kernels/xdna2-moe.cc`, `overlays/designs/moe_{experts,manifest}.py`, `xdna-gemv.{h,cpp}`, `xdna-moe.{h,cpp}`)**:
    - In `Q4_K_XL`, `down_exps` (`[640, 2560, 512]`) stores `25.20 GiB` in `Q5_1` (`40` layers) + `4.98 GiB` in `Q8_0` (`8` layers), while `gate/up_exps` (`[2560, 640, 512]`) have `N = 640` (`1.25` blocks of `512` outputs) and `down_exps` has `K = 640` (`2.5` tiles of `256` inputs). Without native `Q5_1` and partial-tile shapes, `xdna2-moe` padded `N = 640 -> 1,024` (`+60 %`) and `K = 640 -> 1,024` (`+60 %` in `bfp16`), inflating each MoE layer to `3.020 GB` in `Q4_K_XL` and `4.380 GB` in `Q8_0`.
    - **`XdnaGemvFormat::cQ5_1 = 5` (`cPieceQ5_1 = 1,536 B` = `0.75 B/weight`)**: packs 8 `block_q5_1` structures (`d, m` fp16 scales in `256 B`, high bits in `256 B`, low nibbles in `1,024 B`), sharing the single `fRunK<1..3>` loop with `cQ4K`/`cQ5K` (keeping Core 0 `.text` within 16 KiB) and using 32-byte-aligned vector scale loads (`vPair * 32` + `aie::shuffle_down(vPairD, 8)`). `GGML_TYPE_Q5_0` rows convert losslessly on load via `fConvertRowsQ5_0ToQ5_1` (`m = fp16(-16 * d)`).
    - **`cParamTailGroups = 9` (`1..4` 8-output groups in the last N block)** and **symmetric `cParamTailSubs = 10` (`2, 4, 6, or 8` 32-input sub-blocks per core in `cQ8_0` and `cQ5_1`, `fXdnaGemvSubPiece`)**: `N = 640` runs as `vBlocks = 2, vTailGroups = 1` (`512 + 128 = 640`, zeroing unused `pSums` lanes and replacing `pIndex / pGroupsOut` with shifts/subtractions to avoid `__udivsi3` on AIE2P; guarded to `>= 4,608 B` per block so multi-block `range_(pParams[13])` object loops never share a half-object), and `K = 640` splits symmetrically across Core 0 and Core 1 as `320 + 320` (`256 + 64` per core: `384 B` tail piece in `Q5_1`, `544 B` in `Q8_0`). Core 0 ELF = **`15,832 / 16,384 B`** (`552 B` free); Core 1 ELF = **`13,240 / 16,384 B`**.
    - Single-pass `llama-xdna-gemv` checks against 64-bit float references: `--moe --moe-hidden 640 --format q5_1` **`0` error (`0.742 ms`)**, `--moe --format q5_1 --moe-switch` **`0` error (`0.614 ms`)**, `--moe --moe-hidden 640 --format q8_0` **`2.94e-08` max error (`0.861 ms`)**, and `--moe --format q6k` **`8.26e-08` max error (`0.637 ms`)**.
    - Per-layer MoE `BO_SHARE` memory dropped **`3.020 -> 1.736 GB` (`-42.5 %`, `92.7 %` of GGUF's `1.610 GB`)** on `Q4_K_XL` and **`4.380 -> 2.718 GB` (`-37.9 %`, `98.0 %` of GGUF's `2.665 GB`)** on `Q8_0`, cutting per-layer NPU decode latency from `1.425 ms` to **`~0.83 ms/layer`** (`48.9 ms/layer` in `pp512` across `5,120` routed slots). On `Q4_K_XL`, `llama-perplexity` (`doc/XDNA2.md`, `-c 512 -b 512 --chunks 1`) improved from `8.6543` (`bfp16` fallback) to **`8.2085 ± 1.5238`**.
 5. **Budget tuning (`vMoeRatio = 0.78`, `vHeadroom = 4.6 GB` when `MemTotal >= 58 GB`) and MGLRU / storage behavior**:
    - With `61.47 GiB` (`66.0 GB`) of RAM, `vMoeWeightsLimit = 50.2 GB` (`4.6 GB` dense headroom) places **100 % of all 48 layers' 2D dense projections** (`0` refused) alongside **`23 / 48` MoE layers (`40.0 GB`)** on `Q4_K_XL` and **`16 / 48` MoE layers (`43.5 GB`, folding `15 / 16` shared experts as virtual expert `#512` on `blk.0` and `blk.2..15`)** on `Q8_0`, with `~15–17 GB` free/page-cache RAM and `0` swap.
    - Because active trunk weights (`77.3 GB` in `Q4_K_XL`, `129.0 GB` in `Q8_0`) exceed physical RAM (`66.0 GB`), `pp512` and `tg64` have opposite page-cache sensitivities on fast local NVMe: in `pp512` every layer touches ~100 % of its 512 experts (Linux 7.3's scan-resistant MGLRU pins what fits and streams the overflow), so placing more MoE layers on XDNA2 (`23/48`) lifts steady-state `pp512` (`52.8 -> 63.5 t/s`, `68.4 t/s` peak), whereas in `tg64` leaving ~`25 GB` of DDR5 page cache (`GGML_XDNA_WEIGHTS_GB=42`, `17/48` MoE layers) caches more hot CPU fallback experts and pushes steady-state `tg64` to **`5.58 t/s`** (`5.70 t/s` peak). On `Q8_0` over USB `5 Gbps` (`~465 MB/s`), ~`60.7 GiB` exceeds physical RAM in both NPU and CPU modes (`133–134 s` sequential USB read in `pp512`), while pinning `16` MoE layers + all `48` 2D dense layers in NPU `BO_SHARE` edges ahead in `tg64` (`0.58` vs `0.56 t/s`).
