 6. **Multi-Token Prediction (`--mtp 1`, `blk.48`) on `Qwen3.8 Flash Next Q4_K_XL` (`src/models/qwen4exp.cpp`, `src/models/qwen35{,moe}.cpp`, `src/llama-context.cpp`, `src/llama-model.cpp`, `ggml-xdna.cpp`, `examples/speculative-simple/speculative-simple.cpp`)** (2026/11/22):
    - **`kv_only` (`n_outputs == 0`) catch-up and `n_vocab_mtp` partial head slicing in `qwen4exp::graph_mtp`**: prompt and deferred catch-up (`flush_deferred`) batches on `ctx_dft` now store only `(Kcur, Vcur)` and QSA indexer `(k_raw, pooled_new)` (`if (q_idx == nullptr) return nullptr` in `build_qsa_sel`), skipping `wq`, `index_q_proj`, `lightning_indexer`, `top_k_mask`, QSA MHA, `wo`, `hc_ffn`, the 512-expert `blk.48` MoE block, `hc_head` and `model.output` (~`37×` fewer bytes per catch-up token), and draft steps slice `model.output` to `cparams.n_vocab_mtp = 65,536` rows (`head_part`, `cb(cur, "mtp_logits_part", -1)` in `qwen4exp`, `qwen35` and `qwen35moe`, cutting draft logit projection traffic `3.79×`).
    - **Zero-offset prefix `VIEW`s and MTP MoE layer reservation on XDNA (`ggml-xdna.cpp`, `src/llama-context.cpp`, `src/llama-model.cpp`)**: `graph_get_cb` routes `MUL_MAT` nodes whose `src[0]` is a zero-offset contiguous prefix `VIEW` (`head_part`) of an XDNA weight tensor to `XDNA`, and `ggml-xdna.cpp` caches them distinctly from the full `248,320`-row `output.weight` via `cWeightViewKeyTag = uintptr_t(1) << 62` (`fPushWeightKey`, `fGivesLogits`, `fWeightTensorBase`, `fInBuffer`), running `head_part` on `xdna2-moe` with `0` overlay switches. `XdnaPlacement::vDummyBuffers` ignores `common_fit_params`'s zero-size probe buffers until freed, `ml.init_mappings(!oversized_ra && !exceeds_ram)` avoids thrashing `78.6 GiB` of sequential `POSIX_MADV_WILLNEED` into `61.5 GiB` of RAM at startup (`load_tensors` startup drops ~`22×`), and when `ml.load_mtp && hparams.n_layer_nextn > 0` (`GGML_XDNA_MTP_FIRST_LAYER=48`, `GGML_XDNA_MTP_LAYERS=1`, `GGML_XDNA_NVME_PARTIAL_CACHE=1`), `fPlaceMoeWeights` uses `84 %` of `vMoeWeightsLimit` (`43.2 GB`) and reserves `vRemDense + vMtpLayerUpper` so `17 / 48` trunk MoE layers plus **`blk.48` (`Q8_0`, `512` routed experts + folded shared expert `#512`)** stay pinned in `BO_SHARE` alongside ~`24 GiB` of clean DDR5 page cache (`~7.7–8.3 ms` total per draft step = `< 3.5 %` of decode wall time). Because `qwen4exp.nextn_predict_layers = 1`, `--mtp 1` (`80–82.9 %` acceptance on RISC-V prose, `71.47 %` mean across 5 diverse technical domains) outperforms self-chained `--mtp 2` (`34.4 %` at position 1).
    - **Why XDNA2 turns MTP into a speedup on `>RAM` NVMe MoE while CPU MTP regresses**: in `M = 2` verification, 2 tokens route ~`19.8` distinct experts per layer (~`2×` the sparse `84 KB` NVMe page faults of `M = 1`), and on CPU `blk.48` (`2.72 GB` `Q8_0`) also competes in the `59 GiB` page cache (`5.18 -> 4.78 t/s` across unseen prompts). Pinning `blk.48`, `17` trunk MoE layers, all `49` dense layers and `output.weight` / `head_part` in XDNA `BO_SHARE` eliminates all NVMe I/O on the draft block and early trunk while running at `70 GB/s`, lifting cross-prompt steady-state speed to **`5.78 t/s` (`1.21×` CPU MTP, `+8.2 %` over non-MTP NPU `5.34 t/s`)** and warm-context speed to **`8.13 t/s` (`+52.2 %` over non-MTP)** with **100 % token-for-token greedy parity** between NPU and CPU:

| Configuration (`llama-speculative-simple -n 64 --temp 0 -t 8 -fa on --mtp 1`) | MoE on NPU | All passes (t/s) | Warm Passes 2..N (t/s) | Mean draft accept |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **NPU Round A1** (`--runs 5 --prompt-cycle`, 5 distinct domains) | `17 / 48 + blk.48` | **6.05** (`5.67–6.29`) | **6.05** | 71.47 % |
| **CPU Round B1** (`-dev none -ngl 0 --runs 5 --prompt-cycle`) | `0 / 49` | 4.60 (`3.89–5.11`) | 4.78 | 71.47 % |
| **NPU Round A2** (`--runs 5 --prompt-cycle`, 5 distinct domains) | `17 / 48 + blk.48` | **5.47** (`4.45–6.22`) | **5.51** | 71.47 % |
| **CPU Round B2** (`-dev none -ngl 0 --runs 5 --prompt-cycle`) | `0 / 49` | 4.85 (`4.34–5.21`) | 4.78 | 71.47 % |
| **2-Round Interleaved Mean (`A1+A2` vs `B1+B2`)** | — | **5.76 vs 4.73 (`1.22×`)** | **5.78 vs 4.78 (`1.21×`)** | **71.47 % (exact parity)** |
| **NPU Warm Single-Prompt** (`--runs 4`, `100 %` page-cache hit) | `17 / 48 + blk.48` | 7.39 | **8.07** (peak **8.13**) | 80.00 % |

 7. **Multi-Token Prediction (`--mtp 1`) across the full GGUF catalog (`ggml-xdna.cpp`, `examples/speculative-simple/speculative-simple.cpp`)** (2026/11/22):
    - **Chaining `shared_head_norm + head_part` inside `fFeedForward` (`ggml-xdna.cpp`)**: in `qwen35::graph_mtp`, a single-row identity `GGML_OP_GET_ROWS` sits between `shared_head_norm` (`vM2`) and the `65,536`-row prefix view `head_part` (`mtp_logits_part`). `fFeedForward` now steps across a single-consumer `fRowsTaken` `GET_ROWS` (`vM2Rows`) when collecting `aNext`, chaining the entire `ctx_dft` (`M = 1`) MTP feed-forward block `{attn_output + residual + RMS_NORM + {ffn_gate, ffn_up} + SwiGLU + ffn_down + residual + shared_head_norm + head_part}` into **one `xdna2-gemv-chain` command** (`180 / 180` chained with logits on `0.8B`, `0` host `GET_ROWS`, `0` overlay switches).
    - **Pure CPU baseline switch (`--no-xdna` in `speculative-simple.cpp`)**: because `XDNA` registers as `GGML_BACKEND_DEVICE_TYPE_ACCEL` (placed ahead of `CPU` inside `make_cpu_buft_list`), passing `-dev none -ngl 0` alone still offloads models with `use_extra_bufts == true` to `XDNA`. `llama-speculative-simple` now accepts `--no-xdna`, setting `GGML_XDNA_DISABLE=1` before `llama_backend_init()` so CPU rounds run 100 % on Zen 5/5c.
    - **2-round interleaved results across all MTP models in `/home/nipegun/IA/Modelos/GGUF/`** (`llama-speculative-simple -c 2048 -fit off -n 64 --temp 0 -t 8 -fa on --mtp 1 --runs 5 --prompt-cycle`, 5 distinct technical domains, AC power):

| Model (`nextn_predict_layers = 1`) | Non-MTP `tg64`, NPU / CPU | NPU MTP (`A1`, `A2` -> mean `2..5`) | CPU MTP (`B1`, `B2` -> mean `2..5`) | NPU MTP vs NPU `tg64` | NPU MTP / CPU MTP | Mean accept (NPU / CPU) |
|---|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `Qwen3.5-0.8B-MTP Q8_0` (`24+1` dense) | 64.62 / 58.35 (`1.11×`) | 61.43, 61.25 -> **61.34 t/s** | 63.68, 65.68 -> **64.68 t/s** | −5.1 % | 0.95× | 77.9 % / 73.4 % |
| `Qwen3.5-2B-MTP Q8_0` (`24+1` dense) | 31.30 / 23.36 (`1.34×`) | 37.19, 36.22 -> **36.71 t/s** (peak **40.47**) | 27.26, 28.60 -> **27.93 t/s** | **+17.3 %** | **1.31×** | 70.3 % / 70.0 % |
| `Qwen3.5-4B-MTP Q8_0` (`32+1` dense) | 14.90 / 10.82 (`1.38×`) | 17.70, 19.41 -> **18.55 t/s** (peak **20.82**) | 14.02, 14.16 -> **14.09 t/s** | **+24.5 %** | **1.32×** | 69.3 % / 73.5 % |
| `Qwen3.5-9B-MTP Q8_0` (`32+1` dense) | 7.42 / 5.61 (`1.32×`) | 12.52, 12.58 -> **12.55 t/s** (peak **13.06**) | 7.99, 7.93 -> **7.96 t/s** | **+69.1 %** | **1.58×** | 79.6 % / 75.4 % |
| `Qwen3.8-27B-MTP Q6_K` (`64+1` dense) | 3.02 / 2.15 (`1.40×`) | 4.27, 4.24 -> **4.25 t/s** (peak **4.55**) | 3.36, 3.18 -> **3.27 t/s** | **+40.7 %** | **1.30×** | 81.9 % / 85.7 % |
| `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-MTP Q6_K` (`40+1` MoE) | 18.02 / 15.73 (`1.15×`) | 18.18, 17.93 -> **18.05 t/s** (peak **19.90**) | 16.68, 17.19 -> **16.93 t/s** | +0.2 % | **1.07×** | 66.5 % / 65.6 % |
| `Qwen3.8-Flash-Next Q4_K_XL` (`48+1` MoE, NVMe) | 5.34 / 5.18 (`1.03×`) | 6.05, 5.51 -> **5.78 t/s** (warm **8.13**) | 4.78, 4.78 -> **4.78 t/s** | **+8.2 %** | **1.21×** | 71.5 % / 71.5 % |

    - **Scaling behavior across architectures and sizes**:
      1. **Dense `2B` to `27B` break through the single-token LPDDR5x bandwidth roof**: in a dense model, verifying `M = 2` tokens reads the exact same weight bytes once (`1.00×` of `M = 1`), while drafting `M = 1` reads only `1 / n_layer` (`1.5–4.2 %`) of the trunk plus `head_part`. On `Qwen3.5-9B Q8_0`, MTP lifts XDNA2 from `7.42` (`69.0 GB/s`) to **`12.55 t/s` (`+69.1 %`, `1.58×` CPU MTP, `116.7 GB/s` effective weight throughput)**; on `Qwen3.8-27B Q6_K` (`65 / 65` layers cached within `vDenseTotalLimit`), XDNA2 climbs from `3.02` (`68.9 GB/s`) to **`4.25 t/s` (`+40.7 %`, `1.30×` CPU MTP, `97.0 GB/s` effective throughput)**.
      2. **Sub-1B fixed command overhead (`0.8B`)**: without MTP, `0.8B` (`~800 MB`) already finishes a token in `15.5 ms` (`64.62 t/s`) using `24` chained commands (`fFeedForward` matches `M = 1` only). In `M = 2` verification, each layer splits into `4` `mChainMultiplyRows` commands (`97` commands per verification step), adding ~`6–7 ms` of fixed `ioctl`/`mWait` overhead (attempting to specialize `fStageWords<5>` in `kernels/xdna2-gemv-chain.cc` overflowed Core 0's `16,384 B` `.text` by `64–80 B` and `fStage` itself is `< 0.1 ms`).
      3. **256-expert Sparse MoE (`Qwen3.6-35B-A3B`)**: unlike dense models where `M = 2` reuses 100 % of streamed weights, two tokens in a `256`-expert `top-8` MoE activate ~`15–16` distinct routed experts per layer (~`1.85×` the expert bytes of `M = 1`), so MTP yields a modest average gain (`18.05 t/s` on NPU vs `16.93 t/s` on CPU, peaking at **`19.90 t/s`** when draft acceptance hits `80 %`).

 8. **Full multi-quantization (`Q4_K_M`..`Q8_0`) and `--mtp 1` sweep across all 57 SMB share GGUF models, `--no-xdna` in `llama-bench`, and `9..39`-row `dMoeMatrices` OOM fix (`ggml-xdna.cpp`, `tools/llama-bench/llama-bench.cpp`, `_/Benchmarks.html`)** (2026/11/23):
    - **Pure CPU switch in `llama-bench` (`--no-xdna`)**: matching `llama-speculative-simple`, `tools/llama-bench/llama-bench.cpp` now accepts `--no-xdna` before `ggml_backend_load_all()`, setting `GGML_XDNA_DISABLE=1` so `-dev none -ngl 0` CPU baselines do not route `use_extra_bufts` weights onto the `ACCEL` `XDNA` device.
    - **Fixing duplicate `BO_SHARE` expert allocation on `9..39`-token batches (`ggml-xdna.cpp`)**: during warmup (`1..8` rows, `fByRows == true`) and `pp512` (`>= 40` rows), `fMulMatTaken` accepted `ffn_*_shexp` and `fEnsureMoeWithShared` cached `257`-expert `BO_SHARE` matrices (`256` routed + `1` virtual shared expert, ~`36.8 GB` on `Qwen3.6-35B Q8_0`) while dropping the 3D `mmap` pages with `MADV_DONTNEED`. On intermediate `9..39`-token prompts (such as the `32`-token prompt of Run 1 in `llama-speculative-simple`), `fByRows` (`<= 8`) and `vCount >= vMinRows` (`>= 40`) were both `false`, so `fMulMatTaken` rejected `ffn_*_shexp`, `fMatchMoeSharedExpert` failed to fold the shared expert, and `fEnsureMoeWeights` looked up the 2-tensor key `{gate_exps, up_exps}` (`256` experts) instead of `{gate_exps, up_exps, gate_shexp, up_shexp}`, re-faulting `35 GB` from disk (`9.2–35.1 s` stall on `Q4_K`..`Q6_K`) and attempting to allocate a second `36.6 GB` `BO_SHARE` copy (`73.4 GB > 66.0 GB` RAM -> kernel OOM on `Q8_0`). Two complementary fixes close both directions: (a) `fMulMatTaken` (`vShexpOnMoe`) keeps shared-expert and shared-gate multiplications on `XDNA` for `9..39` rows whenever `fMoeActiveForRows` and `fLayerHasNpuMoe` hold, folding them into `xdna2-moe` in `<= 24`-row chunks with `0` extra commands (`32`-token prompt encode drops from `35.1 s` on `Q6_K` and OOM on `Q8_0` to **`0.27–0.42 s`**); and (b) `fEnsureMoeWithShared` reuses any existing `(routed + shared)` `dMoeMatrices` entry (`vExperts > vBaseExps`) when queried with `aShexp.empty()`, guaranteeing `0` duplicate `BO_SHARE` allocations in any batch order.
    - **Complete multi-quantization benchmark sweep (`62` rows with sortable `Size` (`GiB`) column in `_/Benchmarks.html`)**: copying each of the 27 remaining SMB models to local NVMe, measuring `llama-bench` (`pp512` / `tg64`, `-r 3 --delay 2 -t 8 -fa 1`) and 2-round interleaved `llama-speculative-simple` (`--mtp 1 --runs 5 --prompt-cycle`, warm runs `2..5`), and deleting each staged copy after measurement completed all `Q4_K_M`/`Q4_K`, `Q5_K_M`/`Q5_K`, `Q6_K` and `Q8_0` families:

|---|---:|---|---|---|---:|

### Faster prompt recurrence (`1a + 1b + 1c`), in-core `Q8_0`/`Q6_K` prompt overlay (`work/bank-comp8`), `XDNA_State` KV cache and single-subgraph execution (`GGML_XDNA_FULL`)

Since 2026/11/25. All benchmarks in this section follow the mandatory AC thermal/power-spike protocol recorded in `RulesForThisProjectOnly.md`: one invocation at a time on AC power (`AC/online = 1`), `-r 3`, and 8 to 10 seconds of idle cooldown between runs.

 1. **Faster prompt recurrence (`1a + 1b + 1c`) and zero recurrent scheduler splits (`xdna-gemm.{h,cpp}`, `xdna-gdnp.{h,cpp}`, `xdna-runner.{h,cpp}`, `ggml-xdna.cpp`)**:
    - **`1a` — Direct GDN `bf16` + L2-norm prepacking while reading `C` of `attn_qkv` (`fConvStrips`, `XdnaGdnPrepack`, `mPrepareGdnB`, `mRunGdnPrepacked`)**: while unpacking the `bf16` strips of `attn_qkv` and computing the 4-tap short convolution + `SiLU` in L1 cache, `fConvStrips` now computes the `q` and `k` L2 norms and writes `k`, `q` and `v` directly into the `bf16` chunk layout of `xdna2-gdn-prompt`, skipping the intermediate `SILU` float write+re-read (`~12–38 MB` per layer at `M = 512`).
    - **`1b` — Direct gated RMS norm from NPU output into `A` of `ssm_out` (`fXdnaGdnUnpackGatedToA`, `mRunGdn`)**: when `GATED_DELTA_NET` feeds `fGatedNorm` → `ssm_out`, `mRunGdn` normalizes each `128`-wide head output, multiplies by `ssm_norm` and `silu(z)` / `sigmoid(z)`, and encodes `bfp16` straight into the `64 × 128` tiles of `ssm_out`'s `A` buffer without writing or re-reading the float output tensor.
    - **`1c` — Per-part overlapped L2 scales and zero recurrent scheduler splits (`fStateOpTaken`, `fStateOpOnHost`)**: when `1a` does not prepack, `mRunGdn` computes L2 scales per part overlapped with the preceding NPU command; claiming `SCALE(0)`, `GET_ROWS` and `CPY` on `XDNA_State` recurrent cells eliminates all recurrent scheduler splits (`Qwen3.5-0.8B` prompt splits drop from `88` to `26`, `0` recurrent splits). `GGML_XDNA_NO_GDN_FOLD=1` turns `1a + 1b` off for A/B comparison.
    - **Precision**: byte-for-byte identical logits on `Qwen3.5-0.8B BF16` (`-b 512` and `-b 200`) against pre-change NPU logits, and byte-for-byte identical 128-token greedy text on `Qwen3.5-4B Q8_0` (`220` prompt + `128` generated tokens).
    - **2-round interleaved `-r 3` A/B speed (`llama-bench -p 512 -n 0 -r 3 -t 8 -fa 1`, `GGML_XDNA_NO_GDN_FOLD=1` vs default `1a + 1b + 1c`)**:

| Model | Baseline (`NO_GDN_FOLD=1`, R1 / R2) | Faster GDN (`1a+1b+1c`, R1 / R2) | Gain | vs Pure CPU `pp512` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Qwen3.5-0.8B Q8_0` | 4,046.43 / 4,078.88 t/s | **4,461.05 / 4,457.24 t/s** | **+9.8 %** | **6.19×** (`720.2`) |
| `Qwen3.5-4B Q8_0` | 1,524.19 / 1,520.03 t/s | **1,650.31 / 1,651.28 t/s** | **+8.5 %** | **16.40×** (`100.7`) |
| `Qwen3.5-9B Q8_0` | 1,114.66 / 1,116.20 t/s | **1,190.30 / 1,190.27 t/s** | **+6.7 %** | **23.12×** (`51.5`) |
| `Qwen3.8-27B Q6_K` (with `vDenseTotalLimit = 0.72`) | 115.70 t/s (2026/11/20) | **210.60 / 209.76 t/s** | **+81.7 %** | **5.81×** (`36.2`) |
| `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B Q6_K` | 381.77 / 380.63 t/s | **392.54 / 392.20 t/s** | **+2.9 %** | **2.16×** (`182.0`) |

 2. **In-core compressed `Q8_0` and `Q6_K` 4-in-3 in the prompt overlay (`work/bank-comp8`: `kernels/xdna2-mm-bfp16-s3.cc`, `overlays/designs/{bfpmm_resident,overlay_manifest}.py`, `overlays/build-overlays.sh`, `xdna-{overlay,gemm,runner,insts}.{h,cpp}`, `ggml-xdna.cpp`, `tools/xdna-gemm/xdna-gemm.cpp`)**:
    - **Design and 16 KiB program fit**: `bfpmm_resident.py` (`--comp8 1`, manifest key `comp 1`) adds runtime dispatch in `xdna2-mm-bfp16-s3.cc` for `XdnaGemmFormat::cBfp16` (`9,216 B` per `64 × 128` `B` tile), `XdnaGemmFormat::cQ8_0` (`8,704 B`/tile: `512 B` `bf16` scales + `8,192 B` `uint8` quants, padded to a `9,216 B` DMA object) and `XdnaGemmFormat::cQ6K` (**`6,912 B`/tile = `0.84375 B/weight`**, packing **4 consecutive K-tiles into 3 `9,216 B` DMA objects** via a 4-phase core object state machine so shim `MM2S` transfers drop by **exactly 25 %**). Both `xdna2-mm-bfp16-s3.cc` and `xdna2_gdn_kq` compile at full **`-O2`**, totaling **`16,320 / 16,384 B`** of `.text` (`64 B` spare) and `< 3,000 / 3,840 B` of stack (`fPrepQ8Tile` / `fPrepQ6Tile` marked `noinline` with scratch on core stack).
    - **Why `bfp16` stays 4.6–6.4× faster than in-core `Q8_0`/`Q6_K` on AIE2P**: on AIE2P, vector integer `mac`/`msc` and matrix `mac_8x8_8x8T` share the same `accfloat` accumulator file (`am0..am4`), so `Q8_0`/`Q6_K` cannot overlap vector dequantization with `bfp16` matrix-multiply in software-pipelined lockstep and must materialize a `9,216 B` `bfp16` tile via `to_v16bfp16ebs8` first; moreover, all 8 cores of a column broadcast and redundantly decode the same `64 × 64` `B` tile (`8×` redundant vector work vs `1×` in `xdna2-gemv-chain`).
    - **Exactness and benchmarks (`llama-xdna-gemm 512 × 4096 × 4096 --repeat 3` and `llama-bench -p 512 -n 0 -r 3 -t 8`)**:
      - `llama-xdna-gemm`: `bfp16` **`65,536 / 65,536` exact** (`0.737 ms`, **`23.31 TOPS`**), `q8_0` **`65,536 / 65,536` exact** (`3.400 ms`, **`5.05 TOPS`**), `q6k` **`65,536 / 65,536` exact** (`4.707 ms`, **`3.65 TOPS`**), `gdn` **`PASS`** (`0.394 ms` NPU).
      - Greedy 64-token text is **byte-for-byte identical** between `bfp16` and `q8_0` on `Qwen3.5-0.8B Q8_0` and between `bfp16` and `q6k` on `Qwen3.8-27B Q6_K` (`267p + 64n`).
      - Model `pp512` speed: on `Qwen3.5-4B Q8_0`, `bfp16` runs at `1,582.12 ± 5.90 t/s` vs `q8_0` `588.68 ± 1.15 t/s` (`5.85×` CPU); on `Qwen3.5-9B Q8_0`, `bfp16` runs at `1,095.65 ± 5.49 t/s` vs `q8_0` `324.16 ± 1.13 t/s` (`6.29×` CPU); on `Qwen3.8-27B Q6_K`, raising `vDenseTotalLimit` from `0.68` to `0.72` (`47.5 GB` with `MADV_DONTNEED`, keeping `24.4 GB` of prompt layers static in `bfp16` and streaming only `5.4 GB`) reaches **`210.60 ± 1.08 t/s` (`5.81×` CPU)**, whereas `GGML_XDNA_PROMPT_FORMAT=q6k` keeps **all 750 matrices 100 % static in `45.0 GB`** (`-7.45 GB` vs `52.4 GB` static `bfp16`, `0` streaming, `-20.6 %` faster load conversion) at **`69.61 ± 0.02 t/s` (`1.92×` CPU)**. `GGML_XDNA_PROMPT_FORMAT=auto` therefore keeps `bfp16` by default and switches automatically to `Q6_K` 4-in-3 only when an explicit `GGML_XDNA_WEIGHTS_GB` budget cannot fit `bfp16 + chain` without streaming.

 3. **`XDNA_State` (`BO_SHARE`) KV cache, `sCleanStates` flush elision, and single-subgraph execution (`GGML_XDNA_FULL=1` / `2`, `src/llama-kv-cache.cpp`, `src/llama-context.cpp`, `ggml-xdna.cpp`)**:
    - **`XDNA_State` KV cache (`llama_kv_cache_accel_buft` in `src/llama-kv-cache.cpp`, active by default)**: CPU-layer KV caches up to `4 GiB` (including `llama_kv_cache_iswa` on `Flint`) now allocate directly in `XDNA_State` (`BO_SHARE` driver buffers, page-aligned to `4,096 B`), shared directly between XDNA2 and the host (`24.05 MiB` on `0.8B`, `16.06 MiB` on `4B`, `40.31 MiB` on `Granite 3B`, `10.08 MiB` on `Qwen3.6 35B`).
    - **Dirty-state tracking (`XdnaShared::sCleanStates`, active by default)**: `fLinearAttention` records state cell addresses written by the NPU and skips the `2 MB` `fXdnaFlushCache` call on subsequent tokens until any host write (`fStateBuffer{Memset,Set,Copy,Clear}`, `fStateOpOnHost`, `fLinearOnHost`, `fFullOpOnHost`) invalidates `sCleanStates`. On `Qwen3.5-4B Q8_0`, `--xdna-linear` (`GGML_XDNA_LINEAR=1`) narrows its gap against default hybrid execution from `-8.6 %` to **`-6.2 %`** (`13.86 ± 0.02 t/s` vs `14.80 t/s`, **`1.28×` faster than pure CPU `10.82 t/s`**; the remaining `4.8 ms/token` is physical LPDDR5x bandwidth: CPU moves the `2 MB` state in L3/DRAM at `~98 GB/s` vs NPU `2 MB` read + `2 MB` write at `~54 GB/s`).
    - **Single-subgraph execution (`GGML_XDNA_FULL=1` and `GGML_XDNA_FULL=2`, `--xdna-full 1|2` in `llama-bench`)**: `resolve_fused_ops` (`src/llama-context.cpp`) accepts `ACCEL`-claimed fused ops (`accel_on_cpu`), `graph_get_cb` routes `l_last` alongside `norm`, `fMulMatTaken` claims MoE routers (`ffn_gate_inp`) and `ssm_out`/`attn_output`, `fLinearOn` runs `2:1` dense linear-attention layers on `xdna2-gemv-chain` while keeping MoE tokens on `xdna2-moe` (`0` generation overlay switches), `fAttnTaken` runs non-QK-norm prompt attention on `xdna2-gemm-bfp16`, and `fFullOpTaken` / `fFullOpOnHost` execute every remaining graph op (`GET_ROWS`, `DUP`, `CONT`, `CPY`, `ROPE`, `SET_ROWS`, `RMS_NORM`, `ADD`/`MUL`/`DIV`, `SCALE`, `CLAMP`, `UNARY`, `SUM_ROWS`, `SOFT_MAX`, `ARGSORT`, `SSM_CONV`, `GATED_DELTA_NET` via AVX2+FMA `fGdnHead128Avx2`, and `1..39`-token `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` via AVX2+F16C `fDotF32F16Avx2` / `fMadF16Avx2` without unparking worker threads on `M = 1`) inside the single XDNA subgraph (**`splits = 1`** across all `1,250..3,699` nodes, `0` graph reallocations, byte-for-byte identical 64-token greedy text on `0.8B`). `GGML_XDNA_FULL=2` additionally sends `1..39`-token `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` to `xdna2-gemm-bfp16` (`21.89 ± 0.04 t/s` on `0.8B`, confirming `12` overlay switches/token × `~2.5 ms` = `~30 ms/token`):

| Model | Graph nodes (`splits`) | Default (`XDNA_State` KV) `pp512` / `tg64` (t/s) | `GGML_XDNA_FULL=1` (`splits = 1`) `pp512` / `tg64` (t/s) | `FULL=1` `tg64` vs Pure CPU |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| `Qwen3.5-0.8B Q8_0` | `1,359` (`1 / 1`) | **4,267.7 / 65.57** | 4,061.6 / **62.29** (`21.89` in `FULL=2`) | **1.07×** (`58.35`) |
| `Qwen3.5-4B Q8_0` (`4,536/4,536` LA on NPU) | `1,809` (`1 / 1`) | **1,595.4 / 14.80** (`13.86` in `LINEAR=1`) | 1,485.3 / **13.78** | **1.27×** (`10.82`) |
| `Granite4.2-3B Q8_0` | `1,250` (`1 / 1`) | **1,266.4 / 18.39** | 1,061.5 / **17.94** | **1.32×** (`13.54`) |
| `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B Q6_K` (`40` routers + `40/40` MoE on `xdna2-moe`) | `3,699` (`1 / 1`) | **385.4 / 18.09** | 365.0 / **15.71** (`0` gen switches) | **1.00×** (`15.73`) |

 4. **Read-only system diagnostic script (`_/xdna2-lab/diagnosticar_sistema_xdna2.sh`)**: collects kernel `7.3.4` `dmesg` (`reset reason`, `amdxdna`, `s2idle`, `ACPI`), module parameters, AC/battery power telemetry and `pkcs11-eventmgr.service` status (`pam_pkcs11` polling for an unconfigured PKCS#11 smart-card reader; disable with `systemctl disable --now pkcs11-eventmgr.service` when smart cards are unused) without probing USB, audio, video, Thunderbolt or the Quectel modem.

### The overlays in the project

On 2026/11/17 the build folders of the experiments (`_/xdna2/work/…`) and the earlier overlays (`_/xdna2/overlays-*/`) were deleted, keeping only the builds of the three default overlays (since 2026/11/25 `work/bank-comp8`, since 2026/11/20 `work/chain9-q8`, and since 2026/11/21 `work/moe1-q51-tail`, replacing `work/bank-rows`, `work/chain9-la2` and `work/moe1` / `work/moe1-q8`) and the data of `probar_rearme.sh`: the paths of those folders in these notes say where each one was built; to repeat a measurement, rebuild it from the designs.

Since 2026/11/16 the overlays the backend loads are in the project, next to their designs, and CMake puts them next to the programs (the backend looks for them in the folder of the program, or in `GGML_XDNA_OVERLAY_DIR`), so that a build from the repository uses the NPU with nothing more.

 - `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/overlays/`: `xdna2-gemm-bfp16.pdi` and `.manifest` (the overlay of the prompts: bands of A in the memory tiles, the kernel in steps of three with `bfp16`, `Q8_0` and `Q6_K` 4-in-3 `B` decoding (`comp 1`), C in bfloat16 by strips, the recurrence of a batch, buffers moved by banks; 549 KB), `xdna2-gemv-chain.pdi` and `.manifest` (the chained overlay for generating tokens, version 9; 549 KB) and, since 2026/11/19, `xdna2-moe.pdi` and `.manifest` (the mixture-of-experts overlay, version 1; 547 KB).
 - `overlays/designs/`: the designs in IRON and the tools that write their manifests, moved from `_/xdna2-lab/` (whose scripts `compilar_prompt.sh`, `bancos_prompt.sh`, `compilar_chain.sh`, `bancos_chain.sh` and `compilar_moe.sh` call them there now). They find the kernels in `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/kernels/`.
 - `overlays/build-overlays.sh <virtual environment> <output folder>`: builds all three with mlir-aie 1.4.5 and Peano from a Python 3.13 virtual environment, with the recipes they were built with: the overlay of the prompts with `bfpmm_resident.py -M 4096 -K 4096 -N 4096 --b-depth 8 --c-type bf16 --kernel s3 --gdn 1 --c-rows 1 --comp8 1`, its buffers moved by `core_banks.py` and built again with `aiecc` from the MLIR with addresses, and its manifest by `overlay_manifest.py prj columns 64 128 64 --comp8 1`; the chained one with `gemv_chain.py --test la` and `gemv_chain_manifest.py`; and the mixture-of-experts one with `moe_experts.py`, its `x` buffers moved to conflict-free banks by `chain_banks.py` and rebuilt with `aiecc`, and its manifest by `moe_manifest.py`.
 - CMake (`ggml/src/ggml-xdna/CMakeLists.txt`): the target `xdna-overlays` copies the six files into the folder of the programs (`bin/`), and `install` puts them with the programs. With `-DGGML_XDNA_BUILD_OVERLAYS=ON -DGGML_XDNA_MLIR_AIE_VENV=<virtual environment>`, the build runs `build-overlays.sh` into the build folder and copies those instead.

## Performance model

An **estimate**, with three points measured on this laptop from end to end. The first two have int8 sums that wrap around, not usable for a model as they are; the third has exact 32-bit float sums:

 - The official int8 matrix multiplication of mlir-aie, not tuned: 3.9 x 10^12 multiply-accumulates per second (7.8 TOPS), with 22.9 GB/s streamed from host memory over 12 shim channels and 171 multiply-accumulates per streamed byte (see [First run on the laptop](#first-run-on-the-laptop)).
 - The design of this project with linear host buffers and tiles of 128 x 64 x 128, with the same kernel: 1.5 x 10^13 (30 TOPS), with 341 multiply-accumulates per streamed byte (see [Linear host buffers](#linear-host-buffers)).
 - The kernel of this project, block floating point operands, tiles of 64 x 128 x 64 and the bands of A kept in the memory tiles: 1.3 x 10^13 (25.8 TOPS), with 404 multiply-accumulates per streamed byte (see [Bands of A kept in the memory tiles](#bands-of-a-kept-in-the-memory-tiles)).

At 2 operations (one multiply-accumulate) per weight and token, they are about 3900, 15000 and 12900 tokens per second of prompt for a model of one billion weights, if the rest of the model cost nothing.

 - The 58 TOPS are the arithmetic peak with the data already inside the core tiles. A third party reached 56 TOPS that way.
 - The weights of a model do not fit in the array (about 6 MB between memory and core tiles), so they stream from host memory through the shim DMA channels for every batch of tokens.
 - A prompt costs about 2 operations per weight and token. What bounds the speed is how many multiply-accumulates every streamed byte takes part in, and that depends on how much stays inside the array: the running sums are the largest item. zest keeps 512 tokens x 512 outputs in 32-bit sums (1 MB) and gets about 227 multiply-accumulates per streamed byte.
 - With that design, zest read Qwen3-4B at about 600 tokens per second on a Ryzen AI 9 HX 370, which is about 4 TOPS and implies a bandwidth of about 10 GB/s. At that ratio, 58 TOPS would need more than 100 GB/s.

 - Measured here: the transfers from host memory reach about 70 GB/s with the 16 shim channels, and 12.4 GB/s on one. At 171 multiply-accumulates per streamed byte, the ratio of the official example, 70 GB/s would feed 1.2 x 10^13 multiply-accumulates per second (24 TOPS), three times what that example does. The design with linear host buffers reaches it with the same tiles (23.6 TOPS), and goes past it with tiles that stream half the bytes (30 TOPS).
 - The memory of a core bounds the tiles, and the tiles bound the bytes: with tiles of m x k x n and 8-bit data, two tiles of A (m x k) and two of B (k x n) share the 64 KiB of a core with the sums of C (m x n). A goes N / (8 n) times and B M / (4 m) times, so the bytes per multiply-accumulate fall with m and n, while the kernel wants a long k. Sums of 32 bits take four times the memory of 8-bit ones.
 - Every core tile has two input streams. At the 12.4 GB/s measured for one stream, about 7 bytes per cycle each, a kernel reaches the 512 multiply-accumulates per cycle only if every byte that enters a core takes part in about 37 of them or more; the official example gets 32 (tiles of 64 x 64 x 64).
 - For the generation of tokens every weight takes part in one multiply-accumulate per token, so the transfers bound it: at 70 GB/s, a model of 2 GB of weights cannot pass 35 tokens per second through the NPU, whatever the kernel. Only batches (the prompt) reuse every streamed byte.

## What other projects found

 - None of the three implementations speeds up token generation: the NPU reads the prompt, in large batches, and only multiplies by weights.
 - zest: one array program serves every matrix size, because the control code of every size is generated on the host; weights converted once to blocks of eight 8-bit values with a shared exponent (9 bytes per 8 values) and kept in NPU buffers; switching contexts costs about 2.4 ms against 0.1 ms for a command; batches under 1024 tokens lose against the GPU, and so do models narrower than 2048.
 - Above about 26 GB of buffers the NPU returned damaged data without an error (zest, Windows).
 - A command that timed out, followed by a new one, left an NPU unusable until power-off; fixed in `amd/xdna-driver` #1780 (November 2026), not checked on this kernel.
 - Firmware and driver that do not match give `ERT_CMD_STATE_ABORT`.
 - `amd/whisper.cpp` (read on 2026/11/15): no code of the NPU itself. Its encoder calls a closed runtime (`FlexMLRT`, device `stx`) with a cache file compiled beforehand by the VAIML compiler of Ryzen AI (the whole encoder as one subgraph), on Windows only. The demo of `amd/RyzenAI-SW` runs Whisper in BFP16, all the operations of the encoder and 93.4 % of those of the decoder on the NPU.
 - Its cache file for whisper-small (`.rai`, 123 MB, Apache-2.0 in the metadata of its repository; looked at as data on 2026/11/16, nothing of it run): a container of the FlatBuffers kind with the configuration of VAIML (device `stx`; `"aieSingleCoreCompiler": "peano"`, the same open compiler of the cores as this project; preemption enabled; spill buffers of 27.6 MB in host memory), some 108 MB of weights in blocks of 8 bytes and a shared exponent (the bfp16 of this project) and some in bf16, and one xclbin ("unified-2x4x4", 112 KB) whose PDI is only 18 KB for the whole array of 8 x 4 cores, with `"enable_multi_layer": "true"` and configuration blocks named `aie_cdo_elfs_pm.bin` (the programs of the cores) and `aie_cdo_init_reconfig.bin`. Its JSON declares a buffer of control packets of 13 MB for the run (`ctrl_pkt0.bin`), not inside the file (likely made by the runtime when it loads). The reading: AMD does not switch overlays (2.5 ms) but reconfigures the array with control packets while it runs, likely the program memory of the cores too, so the 16 KB of program of a core do not bind it; in an encoder a layer works for milliseconds, while generating a token a whole layer takes about 1.5 ms here, and reloading 16 KB of 32 cores (512 KB) for every layer would cost too much.

## Where the code is

Built with `-DGGML_XDNA=ON`.

| Piece | Path | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Driver interface | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/uapi/amdxdna_accel.h` | The ioctls of the driver, copied from Linux 7.3.4 (Debian 13 does not ship it) |
| Device layer | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-device.h`, `xdna-device.cpp` | `XdnaDevice`: opens the NPU, queries it, creates buffers and contexts, submits commands and waits for them. Checked on the laptop with a real design, whose results match the CPU. `fXdnaFlushCache`: the flush of the `SYNC_BO` of the driver (`clflushopt` between two full barriers), done from the process on the lines given |
| Diagnostic tool | `tools/xdna-info/xdna-info.cpp` (`llama-xdna-info`) | Prints what the driver reports; `--check-buffers` creates, writes, syncs and destroys one buffer of every type. It runs nothing in the NPU |
| Kernel for the core tiles, in steps of three | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/kernels/xdna2-mm-bfp16-s3.cc` | The same multiplication in steps of 8 rows by 24 columns, three sets of sums that let the compiler overlap the steps (80 % of the peak), with another order of the tiles; the kernel of the overlay in use (see [The kernel in steps of three](#the-kernel-in-steps-of-three)) |
| Kernel for the core tiles | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/kernels/xdna2-mm-bfp16.cc` | Matrix multiplication of a tile: operands in block floating point, sums in 32-bit floats; with `-DXDNA2_LAST_TILE`, the last tile of K, which writes C rounded to bfloat16. Built with Peano through mlir-aie, not by CMake yet. Its results match the CPU in one core and on the whole array (see [The kernel of this project](#the-kernel-of-this-project) and [C in bfloat16](#c-in-bfloat16)) |
| Instruction streams | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-insts.h`, `xdna-insts.cpp` | `XdnaInstructions` writes a stream operation by operation; `XdnaShimDma` turns transfers between the host and the shim channels into operations, with the descriptors and the waits where mlir-aie puts them (`mPrepare` and `mStart` apart, and `mFreeDescriptors`, for writing descriptors ahead) |
| Overlay manifest | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-overlay.h`, `xdna-overlay.cpp` | `XdnaOverlay`: where an overlay has its streams, its cores and the memory tiles that keep the bands of A, and whether B comes in halves (`split 2`, with the streams D of the last row of cores), read from a text file written when the overlay is built |
| Plan of a multiplication | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-gemm.h`, `xdna-gemm.cpp` | `fXdnaGemmPlan`: the instruction stream and the buffer sizes of a matrix multiplication on an overlay. Identical to the streams of mlir-aie in 11 cases (see [Instruction streams](#instruction-streams)). `fXdnaGemmLimits`: the largest K and N one multiplication of an overlay can have. `fXdnaPackTileRows`: a tile in the order of rows, which is an object of weights of the overlay for generating tokens. `fXdnaGemmPackSwiglu` and `fXdnaGemmSwigluFits`: the SwiGLU of the C of a gate and an up converted straight into the rows of the multiplication that reads it (ffn_down); `fXdnaGemmPackGatedNorm`: the gated norm of a layer of linear attention into the rows of ssm_out |
| Overlay for generating tokens | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-gemv.h`, `xdna-gemv.cpp` | `XdnaGemvOverlay` (its manifest, `xdna2-gemv 4`: cores per stream, segments, formats), `XdnaGemvFormat` (`bfp16`, `Q4_K`, `Q5_K`, `Q6_K`, `Q8_0`), `XdnaGemvPart` and `XdnaGemvSegment` (up to 4 segments of outputs, each in a format), `fXdnaGemvShape`, `fXdnaGemvPlan` (the parameters of a core in one block write), `fXdnaGemvPackStream` (the pieces of a segment and a stream, from floats or from the blocks of ggml, with AVX2 packing for `Q6_K`), `fXdnaGemvPackW`, `fXdnaGemvPackX`, `fXdnaGemvUnpackY`. See [An overlay for generating tokens](#an-overlay-for-generating-tokens) and the sections after it |
| Kernel for generating tokens | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/kernels/xdna2-gemv-bfp16.cc` | `xdna2_gemv_bfp16` and `xdna2_gemv_load_x`: 1 to 8 rows by a block of 16 outputs (32 with one core per channel), whose weights come as a stream of pieces of bfp16, Q4_K, Q5_K or Q6_K, which it decodes; a piece across two objects is put together in a buffer (see [Q5_K, Q6_K and segments of formats](#q5_k-q6_k-and-segments-of-formats)) |
| Rows tool | `tools/xdna-gemv/xdna-gemv.cpp` (`llama-xdna-gemv`) | Opens the context with both overlays and multiplies random rows on the overlay for generating tokens, with weights in bfp16, Q4_K, Q5_K or Q6_K (`--format`) or in segments of several (`--mix`); compares with the CPU, and its decoding of the formats of ggml with that of ggml. With the chained overlay, the same through it, and `--ffn` the feed-forward block of Qwen3.5 4B in one command (`--ffn-scale` for larger gates; `--next-layer` adds the norm and the projections of the next layer as a fourth phase; `--no-early` writes the descriptors of a phase after the wait for the one before; `--drains-first` queues the drains of a phase before its fills; `--sync-bo` flushes with the driver); `--x-outliers`, `--x-from` and `--blocks-from` give X with large values or the rows and weights of a model, and `--reverse` runs the cases in the other order; `--gdn` the recurrence of a layer of linear attention, `--la` (version 9) the whole layer, and `--moe` (`--moe-single`, `--moe-switch`) the mixture-of-experts overlay, against references in 64-bit floats |
| Mixture-of-experts overlay | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-moe.h`, `xdna-moe.cpp`, `kernels/xdna2-moe.cc`, `overlays/designs/moe_experts.py`, `moe_manifest.py`, `_/xdna2-lab/compilar_moe.sh` | `XdnaMoeOverlay` (manifest `xdna2-moe 1`), `XdnaMoeMatrix` (`[stream][expert][segments][objects]`), `XdnaMoeCommand`, `fXdnaMoePlan` (3-slot ring of shim `MM2S` descriptor pairs per channel, linear `S2MM` drain), `fXdnaMoePackExpertSegment`, `fXdnaMoeEncodeRowBfp16` (with optional AVX2 row `RMS_NORM`), `fXdnaMoeTranscodeYToX`, `fXdnaMoeReadY` (with per-stream residual initialization) and `fXdnaMoeReadYTargets`; run in its own context `XdnaRunner::vMoeContext` (`mLoadMoeMatrix`, `mRunMoe`, `mRunMoeFromKept`) and from `fRunMoeStep`, `fRunDenseMoeFfn` and `fMulMat` in `ggml-xdna.cpp` (see [Routed experts on the NPU](#routed-experts-on-the-npu) and [Dense projections and shared experts of a mixture of experts](#dense-projections-and-shared-experts-of-a-mixture-of-experts)) |
| Chained overlay | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-chain.h`, `xdna-chain.cpp` | `XdnaChainOverlay` (its manifest, `xdna2-gemv 9`: an object of Y per row of a block, phases of the recurrence and phases of a whole layer of linear attention), `XdnaChainMatrix` (the layout of a matrix: K in halves of tiles, up to 4 segments, the blocks of a segment one after the other), `XdnaChainPhase` and `XdnaChainCommand` (the phases of a command, with `XdnaChainRecurrence` for a phase of the recurrence and the buffers of states after the matrices, and the layout of the buffer of the host), `fXdnaChainPlan` (with the descriptors of a phase written before the wait for the one before when they fit, and the fills of a phase queued before its drains), `fXdnaChainPackStream`, the writers and readers of the buffer of the host (`fXdnaChainWriteRecurrence`, `fXdnaChainReadRecurrence` for the recurrence; `fXdnaChainWriteLinear`, `fXdnaChainLinearMatrix` and `fXdnaChainPackLinear` for a whole layer of linear attention, version 9), and `fXdnaChainReads` (what a read of Y takes, the lines to flush). See [The chained overlay](#the-chained-overlay) |
| Kernel of the chained overlay | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/kernels/xdna2-gemv-chain.cc` | Ten functions, each built with its `-DXDNA2_CHAIN_*`: the parameters, the start of a phase, the prologue, the weights of an object (the decoders of `xdna2-gemv-bfp16.cc` for 4 groups of 8 outputs; it stops where a block ends and says so), the sums of core 1 over the cascade, their end in core 0 with the epilogue, a row of the block into an object of Y, and (version 8) a row of the recurrence on each core (`fGdnRow`, with the products of 32-bit floats emulated by hand: `fSplit3`, `fMac3`) and its objects of Y, and (version 9) the convolution, the SiLU, the L2 norms and the gates of a layer of linear attention on core 1 (`xdna2_chain_conv`), their hand-over to core 0 (`xdna2_chain_conv_recv`) and the gated norm at the last row of a head (`fGatedNorm`); with `-DXDNA2_CHAIN_NO_COMPUTE` the pieces are counted, not computed, and the recurrence only moves its data |
| Lab of the chained overlay | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/overlays/designs/gemv_chain.py`, `gemv_chain_check.py`, `gemv_chain_manifest.py`, `compilar_chain.sh`, `tamanos_chain.sh`, `chain_banks.py`, `bancos_chain.sh`, `kl_tokens.py` | The design (`--test single|ffn`, `--compute 0`), its data and check (of version 5), its manifest, a build of the overlay with its manifest into a new folder, the size of the code of every function of the kernel, a build with the buffers of the cores moved to other banks, and the KL token by token between two files of log-probabilities of `llama-perplexity` |
| Lab of the overlay for generating tokens | `_/xdna2-lab/gemv_stream.py`, `gemv_check.py`, `gemv_manifest.py`, `probar_gemv.sh`, `medir_4b_gemv.sh`, `medir_4b_q4k.sh`, `medir_4b_formatos.sh` | The design (IRON, `--format`, `--segments`, `--split`), its data and check (bfp16 and Q4_K), its manifest, a build and check of one size, and the speed and precision of Qwen3.5 4B with it |
| Block floating point | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-bfp16.h`, `xdna-bfp16.cpp` | `fXdnaBfp16Encode` and `fXdnaBfp16Decode`: 8 floats to a block of 9 bytes and back. `fXdnaBfp16EncodeNearest`: many blocks at once, rounded to the nearest, with the same bytes and 20 times as fast; `fXdnaBfp16EncodeNearestNormed`, the same for the rows of an RMS norm by its weights, normalised in the registers. `fXdnaBf16FromFloat`, `fXdnaBf16ToFloat` and `fXdnaHalfToFloat` (fp16, the d and dmin of Q4_K) |
| Threads of the host | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-workers.h`, `xdna-workers.cpp` | `XdnaWorkers`: a few threads, kept between multiplications, that share the conversion of rows and weights and the reading of results; built with OpenMP (as ggml is), the threads of OpenMP that the CPU backend uses (see [The part of the processor](#the-part-of-the-processor)) |
| Linear attention and norms on the processor | `ggml/src/ggml-cpu/ops.cpp` (`ggml_compute_forward_ssm_conv_f32`, `ggml_compute_forward_ssm_conv_f32_by_tokens`, `ggml_gdn_head_128`, `ggml_compute_forward_gated_delta_net_f32`, `ggml_rms_norm_sums_8`), `ggml-cpu.c` (`ggml_cpu_try_fuse_ops`, the work buffer of `SSM_CONV` in `ggml_graph_plan`), `src/models/delta-net-base.cpp` (`build_conv_state`, `by_tokens`) | The short convolution (also with its input by tokens, fused with its `SILU`), the recurrence of Qwen3.5 and `RMS_NORM` on the CPU backend, faster with the same results bit for bit; marked `llama.cpp-xdna`, to keep when porting from upstream (see [The prompt of Qwen3.5 4B: host, processor and overlap](#the-prompt-of-qwen35-4b-host-processor-and-overlap)). The recurrent state updated in its cell of the cache when every sequence goes on in its own (`llm_graph_rs_in_place` and `llm_graph_input_rs::in_place` in `src/llama-graph.{h,cpp}`, `ggml_gated_delta_net_set_state_in_place` in `ggml.h`, `ggml.c` and `ops.cpp`, `build_recurrent_attn` in `delta-net-base.cpp`, `build_layer_attn_linear` in `qwen35.cpp`; refused by `rpc_node_ok`), see "The recurrent states in place" in [The chained overlay](#the-chained-overlay) |
| Multiplication from floats | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-runner.h`, `xdna-runner.cpp` | `XdnaRunner`: opens the NPU with an overlay, converts and keeps weights, and multiplies: converts A, writes the stream, sends the command and reads C (see [From floats to floats](#from-floats-to-floats)); in two steps (`mPackRows` into a buffer of A, `mMultiplyPacked`; 5 buffers of A, of which 3 for rows converted ahead: those of ffn_down from its SwiGLU, `mPrepareRowsA`, `mUnpackSwigluRows`, `mReadyRowsA`, and those of ssm_out from the gated norm, `mPackGatedNormRows`) and without waiting (`mSubmitPacked`, `mFinishPacked`: up to two commands in flight). With the overlay for generating tokens as a second compute unit: `mLoadGemvMatrix` (the members of a matrix, each in its format, `XdnaGemvMember`), `mLoadGemvWeights` (bfp16 from floats) and `mMultiplyRows`; or the chained one, as its manifest says: `mLoadChainMatrix`, `mLoadChainLinear` (the fixed objects of a layer of linear attention), `mChainMultiplyRows` and `mRunChain` (a command of phases with what the host gives each and takes from each, `XdnaChainIo`; the cache flushed from the process on the lines written and read, or with the driver after `mSetUserFlush(false)`; the drains of a phase before its fills after `mSetFillsFirst(false)`) |
| Backend of ggml | `ggml/include/ggml-xdna.h`, `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/ggml-xdna.cpp` | The device `XDNA` of llama.cpp: it takes the multiplications by the matrices of weights of a model, converts and keeps the weights, puts together the ones that multiply the same rows, and runs them on the NPU or, emulating, on the processor (see [The backend of ggml](#the-backend-of-ggml)). Registered in `ggml/src/ggml-backend-reg.cpp`. `src/models/qwen35.cpp` puts the projections of a layer one after the other in the graph for it. Generating tokens with the chained overlay, every layer goes in one command (`fFeedForward`), the last one with the output norm and the logits, through the `GET_ROWS` of one row it takes (`fRowsTaken`); rows that do not fit in one command go in a few (`fRowsPerCommand`). The recurrent states of the layers that run on the processor live in buffers of the driver (`fStateBufferType`, asked for by `src/llama-memory-recurrent.cpp` through `ggml_backend_dev_get_state_buft`). The SwiGLU of a batch goes straight into the rows of ffn_down (`fMulMatGlu`, `XdnaBackend::vPacked`; `GGML_XDNA_NO_PACK_GLU` writes it as floats), and the gated norm of a layer of linear attention into those of ssm_out (`fGatedTaken`, `fGatedNorm`, `fGatedOnHost`; `GGML_XDNA_NO_GATED` leaves it with the CPU backend). The L2 norms of q and k go into the recurrence of a batch, with the gates on the host so that they are one part of the graph (`fL2Taken`, `fL2Fold`, `fL2OnHost`, `fGateTaken`, `fGateOnHost`; `GGML_XDNA_NO_L2`), and `src/llama-context.cpp` gives an accelerator such an RMS_NORM and its SCALE. With `GGML_XDNA_LINEAR`, every layer of linear attention of a token generated goes in one command of one phase (`fLinearTaken`, `fMatchLinear`, `fLinearAttention`; `fLinearOnHost` for what does not make a whole layer) |
| Multiplication tool | `tools/xdna-gemm/xdna-gemm.cpp` (`llama-xdna-gemm`) | Multiplies random matrices on the NPU and compares rows of the result with the CPU; `--also` adds multiplications of other sizes in the same context; `pack` and `unpack` convert files without the NPU; `gdn` runs the recurrence of a prompt with random inputs against a reference in 64-bit floats (`--tokens`, any number; `--parts`, the commands of a layer) |
| Recurrence of a prompt | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/xdna-gdnp.h`, `xdna-gdnp.cpp`, `kernels/xdna2-gdn-prompt.cc`; `fXdnaGdnPlan` in `xdna-gemm.cpp`; `XdnaRunner::mRunGdn` | The `GATED_DELTA_NET` of a batch on the overlay of the prompts (`gdn 1`): `fXdnaGdnPack` and `fXdnaGdnUnpack` write the bands and chunks of a part of the rounds and read its outputs and states (AVX2, the cache flushed per task, empty tokens up to a group of 128; the L2 norms of q and k applied while writing when given, `XdnaGdnIo::vQNorms`; the outputs read by whole rows); `mRunGdn` sends up to `mSetGdnParts` commands at once (see [The chained overlay](#the-chained-overlay), «In the model») |
| Overlays of the backend | `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/overlays/` (`xdna2-gemm-bfp16.*`, `xdna2-gemv-chain.*`, `xdna2-moe.*`), `overlays/designs/` (`bfpmm_resident.py`, `core_banks.py`, `overlay_manifest.py`, `gemv_chain.py`, `gemv_chain_manifest.py`, `moe_experts.py`, `moe_manifest.py`), `overlays/build-overlays.sh`, `ggml/src/ggml-xdna/CMakeLists.txt` | The three overlays the backend loads, built, and how to build them again from their designs and their kernels with mlir-aie and Peano; CMake copies them next to the programs and installs them with them (`GGML_XDNA_BUILD_OVERLAYS` and `GGML_XDNA_MLIR_AIE_VENV` build them instead). See [The overlays in the project](#the-overlays-in-the-project) |
| Attention of a batch | `fAttnTaken` and `fFlashAttention` in `ggml-xdna.cpp`; `fXdnaGemmPackAttnQ`, `fXdnaGemmPackBHalves`, `fXdnaAttnMaskKinds`, `fXdnaGemmPackAttnP`, `XdnaAttnRows` and `XdnaAttnScores` in `xdna-gemm.{h,cpp}`; `fXdnaScoresMax`, `fXdnaScoresExp` and `fXdnaHalvesToFloats` in `xdna-glu.{h,cpp}`; `XdnaRunner::mPackAttnQ`, `mLoadAttnB` and `mPackAttnP`; `llama_context::graph_get_cb` in `src/llama-context.cpp` | `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` of a batch as S = Q·Kᵀ and O = P·V on the overlay of the prompts, with P converted on the host straight into the rows of the second; the keys and values converted from the F16 cache; the heads of keys one after the other with the NPU and the host overlapped (see [The attention of a batch on the NPU, as two multiplications](#the-attention-of-a-batch-on-the-npu-as-two-multiplications)) |
| Stream tool | `tools/xdna-insts/xdna-insts.cpp` (`llama-xdna-insts`) | `gemm` writes the instruction stream of a multiplication; `dump` prints the operations of a stream; `empty` writes a stream with no operation, to measure what a command costs by itself, or with `--writes` many writes, block writes or patches into one register, to measure what operations cost; `gemv`, `gemv-pack` and `gemv-unpack` do the same for the overlay for generating tokens (`--format`, `--segments`); `chain` writes the stream of a command of the chained overlay (one multiplication, or `--ffn`, with `--next-layer` or `--logits` four phases, or `--gdn` and `--la`, the recurrence and the whole layer of linear attention; `--no-early` writes every descriptor right before it is queued, and `--drains-first` queues the drains of a phase before its fills, as before) and prints the layout of its buffers and the buffer descriptors a shim tile uses at most; `moe` writes and inspects the stream of a mixture-of-experts command. It does not touch the NPU |
| Run tool | `tools/xdna-run/xdna-run.cpp` (`llama-xdna-run`) | Runs a design compiled with mlir-aie (its `main.pdi` and `insts.bin`) with input and output buffers taken from files, and times it. It never retries a command that did not complete; it prints the state of the context and the last error of the firmware instead |
| Probe tool | `tools/xdna-probe/xdna-probe.cpp` (`llama-xdna-probe`) | Fixed costs that decide the design: commands one by one, 4 in flight and in chains (`chain`); alternating two contexts with an overlay each (`switch`) or two compute units of one context (`units`, with `XdnaDevice::mConfigureContextUnits`). See [Fixed costs: commands, contexts and overlays](#fixed-costs-commands-contexts-and-overlays) |
| Speech synthesis (`Qwen3-TTS`) | `src/models/qwen3tts.cpp`, `tools/mtmd/` (`mtmd.cpp`, `models/qwen3tts-spkenc.cpp`, `models/qwen3tts-gen.cpp`), `tools/tts/tts.cpp` (`llama-tts`), `common/arg.cpp` (`--tts-instruct`, `--tts-voice`), `vendor/miniaudio/`, `_/port-tools/convert_qwen3tts_to_gguf.py` | `LLM_ARCH_QWEN3TTS` Talker (`Base`, `CustomVoice` and `VoiceDesign`), Qwen3-TTS-only `libmtmd` selecting `XDNA` (`mtmd_select_device`), `<|spk_*|>` voice discovery (`mtmd_gen_audio_get_voice`) and `--tts-instruct` conditioning, 15 standalone 2D codebook heads (`gen_code_head_2d`) chained on `xdna2-gemv-chain`, standalone 2D causal/transposed convolution weights (`conv1d_w_2d`, `conv1d_tr_w_2d`) for `code2wav` on `xdna2-gemm-bfp16`, and the Hugging Face → GGUF converter (see [Speech synthesis: Qwen3-TTS](#speech-synthesis-qwen3-tts)) |

## Open questions

 - What a stream inside the array carries at most, and why: the design with resident bands needs 8.9 GB/s on every stream of a core to run at the rate of its kernel (32 TOPS) and gets 26 TOPS.
 - Why the times of consecutive runs of one design cluster in steps of about 0.45 ms.
 - What sets the rate of a shim channel when many read at once (5 to 7 GB/s each with 12, against 12.4 alone). With all 32 shim channels at once, the multiplications of the model reach about 76 GB/s between reading and writing (see [Weights through eight channels](#weights-through-eight-channels)).
 - How the streams carry more than 32 bits per cycle at 1800 MHz: wider, or faster.
 - Whether a command that hangs in a context of the right width leaves the NPU in the same state as the one that hung in a narrower context.
 - How many tokens and outputs can stay inside the array with 16-bit sums instead of 32-bit ones.

Answered since they were written down:

 - The bandwidth from the array back to the host with all the channels at once: about 55 GB/s with the 16, 45 with 4 (see [Transfers alone](#transfers-alone)).
 - Whether the scores of the attention (S = Q·Kᵀ) can leave the array in bfloat16, as the overlay of the prompts writes C: yes, it adds a KL of some 0.00004 (see [The attention of a batch on the NPU, as two multiplications](#the-attention-of-a-batch-on-the-npu-as-two-multiplications)).
 - Which tiles a kernel for real use needs: 64 × 128 × 64, with the sums in floats in a buffer of their own and C in bfloat16 (see [The kernel of this project](#the-kernel-of-this-project) and [C in bfloat16](#c-in-bfloat16)).
 - How precise the number formats are with a real model: bfp16 costs what Q8_0 costs on the processor, and the weights in Q4_K, Q5_K and Q6_K, read as the file stores them, are as precise as the processor with the same file (see [The backend of ggml](#the-backend-of-ggml) and [Weights in Q4_K for generating tokens](#weights-in-q4_k-for-generating-tokens)).
 - How fast the host converts A and reads C next to the NPU, and what the backend gives with a model: measured with Qwen3.5 0.8B and 4B (see [The backend of ggml](#the-backend-of-ggml), [Qwen3.5 4B](#qwen35-4b) and [The prompt of Qwen3.5 4B: host, processor and overlap](#the-prompt-of-qwen35-4b-host-processor-and-overlap)).
 - The cost of switching between overlays, and whether command chains lower the fixed cost of commands: some 2.5 ms to change overlays, between two contexts or between two compute units of one context; 0.055 ms a lone command, and 0.015 to 0.020 ms with up to 4 in flight or in chains of `ERT_CMD_CHAIN` (see [Fixed costs: commands, contexts and overlays](#fixed-costs-commands-contexts-and-overlays)).
